#2217. Ex-Celtic star backs 'amazing' former boss to deliver amid growing pressure

Vidar Riseth believes Celtic should stick with Martin O’Neill and has backed his former boss to mastermind a huge European result against Besiktas.

The Norwegian played under O’Neill during his first spell at Parkhead and remains convinced the 74-year-old is the right man to steer the Hoops through their current difficulties.

Speaking to SunSport, Riseth insists Celtic are fortunate to have a manager of O’Neill’s calibre in the dugout as he urged the club hierarchy to keep faith with him.

O’Neill is under increasing pressure following six defeats in Celtic’s last ten matches and desperately needs a victory against Besiktas to keep their slim hopes of reaching the Europa League knockout stage alive.

But Riseth has no doubts over his former gaffer’s ability to turn things around. He said: “For Martin to come back how he did last season was amazing. And now the best thing Celtic can do is to keep Martin as long as they can, because he is one of the great coaches.

“It doesn’t surprise me he is still going at his age. As long as Martin can talk and can sit in the dugout, he will do a good job because he’s an amazing guy.

Three Celtic summer signings named in B team XI to face Ross County

Celtic vs Hibs moved to new date as a result of Premier Sports Cup final

Martin O'Neill and Celtic chiefs given ‘hard but fair’ grilling at recent meeting

“Straight away, Martin was such a good leader for us - as soon as he came into the dressing room, you just knew Martin was going to be a great coach.”

Riseth experienced O’Neill’s impact first-hand after the Northern Irishman took charge of Celtic in 2000.

Celtic’s European campaign has been a major source of frustration this season, with Riseth’s former club LASK producing a dramatic turnaround to knock the Hoops out of the Champions League.

Their difficulties have continued in the Europa League, where three defeats have left Celtic 35th in the 36-team league phase. However, Riseth insists there is still time for O’Neill and his players to salvage their campaign.

He said: “After seeing the first leg with LASK, I was sure Celtic were going to win. That was one of the most disappointing games I’ve ever seen from Celtic. They’ve still hopefully got enough games left to turn it around in the Europa League.

“They still have a chance, so they just have to believe and keep going.”

#2215. Dalla Francia: Napoli, spunta il primo nome concreto per il post Allegri. Conceicao è un candidato

Sergio Conceicao entra nel toto-nomi per l'eventuale successione di Massimiliano Allegri sulla panchina del Napoli. L'indiscrezione arriva dalla Francia, precisamente da Foot Mercato, secondo cui l'allenatore portoghese sarebbe uno dei principali candidati nel caso in cui Aurelio De Laurentiis decidesse di cambiare guida tecnica. Per il momento non ci sono decisioni sul futuro di Allegri, ma la pesante sconfitta con il Porto in Champions League e il difficile avvio di stagione lasciano il dubbio.

Il nome di Conceição non è nuovo negli ambienti partenopei. De Laurentiis lo apprezza da diversi anni e avrebbe già provato a portarlo a Napoli in due occasioni, nel 2021 e nel 2023. Entrambe le volte, però, l'operazione si sarebbe arenata di fronte alle richieste economiche del Porto, che pretendeva il pagamento integrale della clausola prevista dal contratto dell'allenatore. Una situazione diversa rispetto a quella attuale: il portoghese, che ha allenato anche il Milan nella seconda parte della stagione 2024/25, è infatti libero e potrebbe accettare immediatamente una nuova destinazione.

Conceição, inoltre, avrebbe recentemente rifiutato la proposta del Fenerbahçe, preferendo aspettare un'opportunità in uno dei cinque principali campionati europei. La Serie A rientra dunque nei suoi programmi, anche dopo la breve esperienza milanista. Per ora si tratta di una candidatura e non di una trattativa avviata: molto dipenderà dalle valutazioni di De Laurentiis e soprattutto dalle prossime prestazioni del Napoli. A cominciare dalla sfida di domenica con la Lazio, quasi da dentro o fuori per Allegri.

#2212. Celtic's record transfer handed surprising ‘doesn’t matter’ goals verdict

Kasper Hogh has been told he can still have a major impact at Celtic even if the goals don’t start flowing.

The £11 million record signing has struggled to reproduce the form he showed at Bodo/Glimt, where he established himself as a major attacking threat.

But former Celtic star Vidar Riseth believes Hogh’s difficulties can partly be explained by the different role he is being asked to perform.

Riseth watched the Danish frontman flourish at Bodo/Glimt and feels his physical presence can be used to create opportunities for the players around him. He said: “Hogh is a big, strong target man. When he played for Bodo, they played a different style that worked for him. [SunSport]

“I think he’d like to get more crosses, as he did at Bodo, because he’s such a big lad with a good finish. Now they’re playing more up to him and asking to get the ball back again, so it’s different for him, but the most important thing for Martin is Celtic winning games.

Ex-Celtic star backs 'amazing' former boss to deliver amid growing pressure

Three Celtic summer signings named in B team XI to face Ross County

Celtic vs Hibs moved to new date as a result of Premier Sports Cup final

“If Martin wants to use Hogh as a way to give more space to other players, then that’s the best way to use him.”

Hogh showed his quality on the European stage during his time at Bodo/Glimt, scoring in Champions League victories over Manchester City and Atletico Madrid.

And Riseth insists the striker does not necessarily have to be the man finding the net to make a difference for Celtic. The Norwegian believes Hogh can occupy defenders and provide the space for his team-mates to make the telling contribution instead.

Riseth added: “Sometimes at Bodo he didn’t score the goals, but there’s always one or two defenders on him all the time. That gives space to the midfield and to other players to come through and score the goals.

“If he works hard and does a good job, it doesn’t matter who scores.”

#2211. Farioli in conferenza stampa: “Il Napoli ha preparato la gara in maniera intelligente, match importante”

Francesco Farioli, allenatore del Porto, ha rilasciato alcune dichiarazioni in conferenza stampa dopo la vittoria in Champions League contro il Napoli:  “Il gioco è stato molto pesante, ci sono stati diversi momenti in cui siamo stati molto energetici e coraggiosi, soprattutto quando siamo andati a prendere la palla e loro ci pressavano, avevamo la giusta mentalità come i tifosi e questo ci ha aiutato ad avere la prestazione avuta. In ogni intervista ho detto che è stata una buona Champions ma non avevamo avuto punti, stavolta abbiamo raccolto i frutti. Questa vittoria è importante perchè ci servivano punti, sappiamo la storia del club e l’esigenza di fare bene in Champions League, di ottenere risultati. Una cosa non si può dire, che ci sia mancanza di volontà: ogni tre giorni abbiamo le giuste motivazioni, ci sono sempre gli avversari e le partite da giocare, e non sempre siamo al top della forma e a certi livelli la pressione si fa sentire. Dobbiamo imparare sulla nostra pelle, ci prendiamo i tre punti contro una squadra eccellente e ora vediamo le ultime quattro partite per guadagnare più punti possibili. La partita di stasera e i risultati hanno cambiato la prospettiva del match, abbiamo dovuto cambiare interpretazione del match e il coraggio dei ragazzi è stato notevole. Siamo stati bravissimi nella verticalizzazione e nel secondo tempo, l’impatto delle sostituzioni è stato fondamentale, chi è entrato ha contributo tanto. Cosa non è andato bene alla fine del primo tempo? In Champions ogni punto conta così come ogni gol può fare la differenza, stasera vincere con tre gol di differenza sicuramente è importante e ci aiuta ad avanzare, la performance dei ragazzi è stata eccellente. Le difficoltà del primo tempo? Non sapevamo cosa avrebbero fatto, avevamo preparato cinque scenari possibili e stasera abbiamo visto le scelte degli avversari con giocatori che non ci aspettavamo in campo. Abbiamo reagito bene e fatto la differenza. Come ho visto il Napoli? Non per dare meriti ai miei, ma il Napoli ha preparato la partita in maniera intelligente, quando ho visto la formazione ho pensato come potessero creare. La prima lettura è stata una scelta strategica e ben pensata in pochi minuti, abbiamo cercato di aggiustare le nuove dinamiche con l’ingresso di De Bruyne sotto punta, siamo stati bravi a portare dentro tanta energia, nel secondo tempo siamo venuti fuori con la condizione fisica e ritmo, chi è entrato ha alzato il ritmo ed è qualcosa in cui credo molto. Ho chiesto a Fofana e Gimenez di giocare fuoriruolo ma hanno avuto determinazione e spirito. Il calcio italiano? Le informazioni sono veloci e si può raffrontarsi con altre realtà, la Serie A la seguo da tifoso e da osservatore esterno, alcune dinamiche le conoscete meglio voi. Nuova onda degli allenatori toscani? Facciamo un lavoro bellissimo quando i risultati vanno bene, difficilissimo se la palla non entra. Spalletti ed Allegri sono dei riferimenti del calcio italiano, hanno portato innovazione nel ruolo dell’allenatore e ho un rispetto enorme per ciò che hanno fatto e continuano a fare. Su Alvini posso dire che è incredibile per lo spirito che ha il Frosinone, tanti anni fa andavo a vedere i suoi allenamenti al Tuttocuoio e ha sempre avuto una grande passione. Mi ha aperto le porte del suo centro sportivo per gli allenamenti e ancora non pensavo di allenare, ha un modo contagioso per come allena. Come abbiamo vinto? A fine primo tempo ho detto di avere ancora fiducia come nei primi 35′ di partita. Abbiamo avuto coraggio e alla lunga abbiamo meritato la vittoria, grande meriti ai ragazzi. Gabri Veiga aveva la febbre, aveva una faccia bianca stamattina e non aveva dormito: quando è uscito gli ho detto meglio fare così ogni partita. Ha grande intelligenza, conosce i momenti della gara e oltre a leggere il gioco aggiunge qualcosa di speciale come la sua fisicità strabordante come volume ed esplosività. Ha ancora grandi margini di miglioramento sulla continuità, sta migliorando e rispetto al suo percorso ha voglia di fare tanto e bene. Poi tra cinque anni chissà, per ora ce lo godiamo. Occasione per un italiano all’estero prima di tornare in Italia? A volte non danno e a volte non si prendono le opportunità, ci sono momenti: negli ultimi due anni e mezzo ho lavorato per club con sale piene di trofei. Lavorare assieme come succede al Porto poi ti rende difficile guardare altrove, sono felice qui: è la realtà, sono felice di lavorare con un gruppo straordinario e con le persone che rappresentano il club e fanno di tutto per il club, creativi nel cercare soluzioni nei momenti di difficoltà. Abbiamo tante partite da giocare, testa bassa e pedalare”.

I Am Naples Testata Giornalistica - aut. #2208. La rete di Pinamonti in Lazio-Cagliari: l'analisi dell'ex arbitro Marco Gabriele

L’ex arbitro Marco Gabriele analizza a livello regolamentare la rete di Andrea Pinamonti contro il Cagliari

L’ex arbitro Marco Gabriele è intervenuto ai microfoni di Radio Radio per esaminare il discusso gol di Andrea Pinamonti, arrivato sul finale di Lazio-Cagliari. L'episodio continua a far discutere poiché la rete del 2-1 è nata mentre Deiola era rimasto a terra dopo una pallonata, con il gioco che è proseguito regolarmente fino alla conclusione vincente dell'attaccante.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

A mio avviso Colombo ha gestito correttamente la situazione e la rete è regolare. Dopo la conclusione di Dele-Bashiru, il pallone è rimasto per alcuni secondi a disposizione dei giocatori del Cagliari, prima che l’azione proseguisse. Questo è un elemento importante nella valutazione dell’episodio.

L’arbitro non è obbligato a interrompere automaticamente il gioco ogni volta che un calciatore viene colpito dal pallone. Lo deve fare quando ritiene che ci sia un infortunio grave. Per questo, secondo la mia valutazione, Colombo ha fatto bene a lasciar proseguire l’azione.

#2207. Celtic learn match officials for Besiktas and Kilmarnock home double-header

Match officials have been confirmed for Celtic’s upcoming clashes with Besiktas and Kilmarnock.

Celtic face the Turkish side at Celtic Park in the Europa League before returning to Premiership duty against Kilmarnock in Glasgow.

German referee Sascha Stegemann will take charge of Celtic’s meeting with Besiktas. He will be assisted by fellow Germans Christof Gunsch and Marco Achmuller, with Martin Petersen appointed as the fourth official. Benjamin Brand will be on VAR duty, assisted by Patrick Hanslbauer.

Stegemann has previously been involved in a Celtic game, although he has not refereed Celtic from the middle. He was in charge of VAR when Celtic drew 0-0 away to Dinamo Zagreb in the Champions League in December 2024.

Celtic's record transfer handed surprising ‘doesn’t matter’ goals verdict

Ex-Celtic star backs 'amazing' former boss to deliver amid growing pressure

Celtic vs Hibs moved to new date as a result of Premier Sports Cup final

Celtic will then turn their attention back to domestic action when Kilmarnock visit Celtic Park on Sunday, November 8.

The Premiership clash kicks off at 4pm, and John Beaton has been appointed as the man in the middle. David Roome and Gordon McCabe will run the lines, with Colin Steven acting as the fourth official. Grant Irvine will oversee VAR, assisted by Dan McFarlane.

It will be Beaton’s second Celtic appointment of the 2026/27 season. His first was the Glasgow Derby against Rangers at Celtic Park.

Last season, Beaton refereed seven Celtic league matches, with Celtic winning five, drawing one and losing one.

Those victories came against Aberdeen, Kilmarnock, Falkirk and twice against Motherwell. Celtic drew 2-2 away to Rangers, while their only defeat was a 2-1 loss at Dundee United.

#2205. Cristiano Ronaldo lascia la Nazionale portoghese: tutte le reazioni

Dopo vent'anni, 234 presenze e 146 gol l'avventura di Cristiano Ronaldo con la maglia della Nazionale portoghese si è chiusa oggi. Ad annunciarlo lo stesso campione con un messaggio pubblicato sui propri canali social.

Di seguito vi proponiamo tutte le reazioni alla decisione dell'ex campione di Manchester United, Real Madrid e Juventus!

Ore 14:43, Jorge Mendes - Tramite X arrivano anche le parole di chi ha accompagnato Cristiano Ronaldo nel corso di tutta la sua carriera, l'agente Jorge Mendes: "Il più grande simbolo del Portogallo e del mondo, ha definito un'era. Lo dico ancora una volta: Cristiano Ronaldo è il miglior giocatore di sempre nella storia del calcio mondiale. Tutti noi portoghesi diciamo: grazie Cristiano. Non ce ne sarà mai un altro come te".

Ore 13:39, Gonçalo Ramos - "Grazie di tutto" è il breve messaggio che Gonçalo Ramos, attuale centravanti del Milan ha voluto lasciare in calce al post con il quale CR7 ha dato l'addio alla Nazionale.

Ore 13:34, Nani - In campo insieme in 130 occasioni, sia con la maglia del Manchester United che con quella del Portogallo, CR7 e Nani sono state una delle coppie di attaccanti più prolifiche delle ultime decadi. Ecco il messaggio che Nani ha voluto rivolvere all'ex compagno: "Congratulazioni, fratello, per questo splendido percorso! Ci hai ispirato tutti e ci hai fatto credere che, come portoghesi, potevamo essere i migliori al mondo. Ci hai resi orgogliosi di noi stessi, come popolo e come squadra. Sei e sarai sempre un punto di riferimento per tutti noi che ti seguiamo e che condividiamo momenti meravigliosi con te, dentro e fuori dal campo. La Nazionale è l'apice delle nostre carriere e sono incredibilmente orgoglioso di aver fatto parte della sua storia. Per me, sei il migliore di tutti i tempi, non solo per il giocatore che sei stato e per tutto ciò che hai dato alla nostra Nazionale, ma anche per ciò che hai rappresentato per tutti noi. Anche quando spesso non eravamo d'accordo, siamo sempre cresciuti, migliorati e abbiamo imparato. E ti dobbiamo anche questo. Per tutto questo, posso solo dire: grazie mille, Capitano! Ciò che hai fatto per il Portogallo e per tutti noi non sarà mai dimenticato".

Ore 13:30, Reinaldo Teixeira - Queste le parole del presidente della Liga Portugal, la massima serie del calcio portoghese, all'addio di Cristiano Ronaldo alla Nazionale: "La parabola di Cristiano Ronaldo, come persona e come calciatore, è da tempo indissolubilmente legata al nome del Portogallo. È - e continuerà ad essere per molti anni - un simbolo della nostra identità culturale e dell'inestimabile valore che apporta a questa passione condivisa da milioni di cittadini in tutto il mondo: il calcio. Il suo addio alla nazionale è una decisione, come tante altre nella sua vita, che ha un profondo impatto su molte persone a livello globale. Non ho dubbi nell'affermare che sia una personalità che trascende paesi e culture, qualcosa di cui noi portoghesi possiamo essere orgogliosi, sempre per il modo in cui si è affermato: come uno di noi. Ciò che ci hai dato non può essere racchiuso in poche righe o commenti. E continuerai a darci. Sono convinto che, pur avendo definitivamente appeso al chiodo la maglia della nazionale, il sentimento e la consapevolezza di essere portoghese rimarranno impressi sulla tua pelle e nelle tue emozioni. Cristiano Ronaldo saprà intrecciare il suo declino da giocatore con l'industria e l'arte che ha lasciato un segno indelebile da quando il suo nome ha iniziato a suscitare meraviglia. La sua storia sarà eterna, così come la nostra gratitudine per ciò che ci ha donato e continuerà a donarci. A nome della Liga Portugal e a nome mio personale, grazie di cuore!"

Ore 13:28, Bruno Fernandes - Arriva, via Instagram anche il post di Bruno Fernandes, compagno di Nazionale di CR7 e attuale capitano del Manchester United: "Grazie per avermi ispirato. È stato un privilegio poter condividere i grandi momenti della nostra Nazionale con il mio idolo d'infanzia".

Ore 13:26, Neymar - Una sola parola è quella che Neymar ha scelto di usare per commentare la notizia dell'addio di CR7 alla Nazionale portoghese in calce al post del fenomeno lusitano: "Leggenda".

Ore 12:50, Pedro Proença - Anche Pedro Proença, presidente della Federcalcio portoghese, ha pubblicato un lungo messaggio indirizzato a Cristiano Ronaldo dopo la notizia del suo addio alla Nazionale Portoghese: "Per il Portogallo, Cristiano Ronaldo sarà più di un semplice giocatore. Un uomo, un atleta, un modello, un eroe, un leader. Il nostro Capitano. Nel giorno in cui annuncia il suo ritiro dalla Nazionale, a nome della Federazione Calcistica Portoghese e di tutto il calcio portoghese, ringraziamo Cristiano Ronaldo per tutto ciò che ci ha dato. E ha dato tantissimo. Più delle vittorie, dei trofei e dei record, più dello status di giocatore più internazionale di tutti i tempi e di miglior marcatore della nostra storia, ciò che rimane è il suo esempio e la sua ispirazione. L'ispirazione di chi ha portato il Portogallo in tutto il mondo. Che, in ogni angolo del pianeta, ogni volta che si parlava del Portogallo, qualcuno nominasse Cristiano Ronaldo. E l'esempio per tutti coloro che sognano di migliorarsi ogni giorno. Come presidente della Federazione Calcistica Portoghese, ringrazio Cristiano Ronaldo per tutto il supporto dimostrato nell'ultimo anno e mezzo. Per le conversazioni e la vicinanza. Le porte della Federazione Calcistica Portoghese saranno sempre aperte per Cristiano Ronaldo. Grazie di tutto e di molto altro ancora. Per Sempre il nostro Capitano".

Ore 12:42, Antonio José Seguro - Arriva anche il messaggio del Presidente della Repubblica portoghese Antonio José Seguro alla notizia dell'addio alla Nazionale di Cristiano Ronaldo: "A nome del Portogallo, ringrazio Cristiano Ronaldo per come ha rappresentato la Nazionale, per aver ispirato il popolo portoghese e per aver portato il nome del Portogallo in tutto il mondo. Immagino che questo sia uno dei giorni più difficili della carriera sportiva di Cristiano Ronaldo, ma sono certo che, come è accaduto tante altre volte, abbia il popolo portoghese al suo fianco. So anche che il Portogallo continuerà a contare sul suo amore per il Paese, che rappresenterà sempre con la dedizione che ha profuso in ogni allenamento, in ogni partita, in ogni gol che ha riempito tutti di gioia. Tantissimi auguri, Cristiano. E grazie di cuore".

Ore 12:31, Katia Aveiro - C'è un velo di polemica nelle brevi parole scritte da Katia Aveiro, altra sorella di CR7 alla notizia della decisione di salutare la Nazionale portoghese da parte dell'ex campione di Real Madrid, Manchester United e Juventus: "Potrei dire tante cose. Ma solo il tempo le dirà. Ve lo dico io. È enorme, è gigantesco. È leggendario. Fino alla fine".

Ore 12:23, Luis Montenegro - Le reazioni alla notizia sull'addio di CR7 al Portogallo superano anche i confini del mondo del pallone. Queste le parole, diffusa via social, di Luis Montenegro, primo ministro del Portogallo: "Grazie! Quello che hai fatto in campo per il Portogallo risuonerà nell'eternità. E ora, fuori dal campo, continuerai comunque a essere il Portogallo nel mondo"

Ore 12:21, Elma Aveiro - "Il più grande orgoglio della mia vita. Ti amo. Sempre con te" è questo il primo post social legato all'addio di Cristiano Ronaldo alla maglia della Nazionale portoghese pubblicato via Instagram da Elma Aveiro, sorella del campione ex di Manchester United, Real Madrid e Juventus.

Ore 12:16, Pepe - Uno dei giocatori che più ha legato la propria carriera a quella di CR7 è stato senza dubbio Pepe. I due fra Nazionale e Real Madrid sono scesi in campo assieme in 348 partite, per un totale di 28.097 minuti fianco a fianco. Per questo quello dell'ex difensore è stato uno dei primi messaggio in risposta all'annuncio dell'addio di Ronaldo alla Nazionale portoghese: "È stato più che calcio. Grazie per tutto Cris".

Ore 12:11, Rio Ferdinand - Una delle prime reazioni alla notizia arriva da Rio Ferdinad, ex compagno di CR7 ai tempi dello United. Poche parole, quelle scritte in calce al post del campione lusitano, ma che danno il senso della carriera vissuta dal classe 1985: "È stato un film fratello".

#2203. USA international has already shown why Stoke City struck gold with £4m summer signing: Opinion

Stoke City picked up another huge win in the week, beating Blackburn Rovers 2-1 at Ewood Park. Summer signing Jack McGlynn caught the headlines after his cheeky free-kick goal, catching Balasz Toth out at his near post.

McGlynn is quickly proving to be one of Stoke City’s shrewdest summer signings, with the American midfielder making an immediate impression under Mark Robins. That free-kick was the latest of several excellent contributions as a he continues to make his mark in England.

It was the 23-year-old’s fourth goal of the campaign and another demonstration of the technical quality that persuaded Stoke to bring him over from Houston Dynamo in September. Even with Rovers boss Tony Mowbray highlighting the player’s ability, they still could not stop him.

McGlynn arrived for a reported fee of around £4m, signing a three-year contract after previously developing with Philadelphia Union before his move to Houston. He was hardly a household name among Championship supporters, but his early performances suggest Stoke identified a player with considerable potential.

The statistics certainly make encouraging reading. McGlynn has registered four goals and two assists in 10 Championship appearances, starting eight matches and accumulating 706 minutes.

His average FotMob average rating of 7.35 underlines his influence, particularly considering his return of four goals has come from an expected goals figure of 0.70. His performance against Preston North End was another example of his attacking contribution.

McGlynn delivered the cross for Ben Wilmot’s opener before scoring himself with a composed left-footed finish in the 2-0 victory. That followed a particularly impressive display in which he demonstrated his ability to influence matches through both delivery and finishing.

There is more to his game than spectacular set-pieces, too. His left foot gives Stoke an additional dimension in advanced midfield and wide areas, allowing him to create opportunities, threaten from distance and provide dangerous deliveries.

His understanding of the Championship’s physical demands will inevitably continue developing, while there is scope for him to become more influential during open-play possession. Nevertheless, Robins already appears to have found an exciting attacking weapon.

McGlynn has the technical ability, confidence and versatility to become a significant player for Stoke, and his early numbers suggest he could even reach double figures for goals and assists this season.

For a reported £4m, the Potters look to have secured an excellent piece of business. McGlynn is already delivering, and there is every reason to believe his best performances are still ahead of him.

#2202. Mandas a Formello: una figurina di Robben con il volto di Isaksen

Il fotomontaggio mostrato su Instagram richiama il gol realizzato dal giocatore biancoceleste contro il Cagliari.

Christos Mandas ha mostrato in un post Instagram una figurina attaccata a un macchinario della palestra di Formello. È un fotomontaggio di Arjen Robben con il volto di Gustav Isaksen, legato al gol segnato contro il Cagliari.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Nell’immagine pubblicata da Mandas, la figurina si trova su uno degli attrezzi della palestra, come racconta Il Messaggero.

Il fotomontaggio riprende Robben ai tempi del Bayern e sostituisce il suo volto con quello di Isaksen.

#2201. Celtic B served an unflinching reality in defeat as big-name kids shown no mercy

Ross County boss Stuart Kettlewell reckons his side could have put Celtic's star-studded B team to the sword by an even bigger margin after booking their place in the next round of the KDM Evolution Trophy with a 2-0 win in Dingwall.

The Staggies boss knew his side would face a stern test against a Celtic second string packed with pedigree. £3million summer signing Joel van den Berg was handed his debut, while highly-rated Chelsea youngsters Landon Emenalo and Shim Mheuka also started.

But County proved too strong for their visitors and Kettlewell believes the scoreline flattered the young Hoops after a dominant display in the Highlands.

Speaking to Ross County TV, Kettlewell said: "It was a competitive game. We were a bit frustrated at half-time because we didn't feel we'd handled the ball particularly well and we didn't think we'd quite got our pressing right.

Read more: Celtic handed familiar whistler for Kilmarnock clash as European ref revealed

"We knew Celtic would be comfortable in possession and would look to retain the ball, but we felt there were more opportunities for us to turn it over.

"That said, we scored a brilliant goal in the first half. The run from Dean Cornelius, the cross from Lewis Jamieson and the finish was first class. We were really pleased with that.

"We also had three, four or five other good opportunities, hit the woodwork a couple of times and, despite not playing as well as we would have liked, still created plenty of chances while limiting Celtic to very little.

"I thought we were better in the second half. We won the ball back more often, had a bit more composure in possession and created a lot of opportunities.

"Their young goalkeeper made some outstanding saves and, on another night, we probably score a few more goals. We hit the woodwork again and had numerous efforts on goal.

"You have to give credit to Celtic. They're a young side, but they're a good side. They've brought in players from elsewhere and have talented homegrown players as well. We always knew it would be a good test and another challenge for us to solve in this competition."

Van den Berg's first outing since arriving from PSV offered an early glimpse of the highly-rated Dutch defender, while Chelsea duo Emenalo and Mheuka both got 72 minutes under their belts.

The trio are not part of Celtic's Europa League squad and therefore won't be involved against Besiktas, but they will hope their contributions in Dingwall have strengthened their case for a place in Martin O'Neill's plans ahead of Sunday's Premiership clash with Kilmarnock.

#2200. Nadal come LeBron e... Al Khelaifi. Chi sono le stelle degli altri sport entrate nel calcio

Alcuni campioni, anche dopo aver smesso di competere in campo, continuano a cercare fuori sfide altrettanto stimolanti. Rafael Nadal, a 40 anni suonati, sembra averne trovata una davvero prestigiosa, questa volta lontano dalla terra rossa. Il fuoriclasse maiorchino è pronto infatti a entrare nella dirigenza del Real Madrid, la squadra per cui non ha mai nascosto la propria simpatia e di cui era già socio onorario. Ieri, 4 novembre, il club di Florentino Perez ha ufficialmente proposto il suo ingresso nella Junta Directiva, il consiglio direttivo che guida le strategie della società merengue: la nomina dovrà essere ratificata dai soci il prossimo 21 novembre. Per uno dei più grandi sportivi di sempre, 22 titoli Slam, 92 tornei vinti e cinque Coppe Davis con la Spagna, si aprirebbe così un capitolo totalmente nuovo.

Quella di Nadal non sarebbe, però, una prima assoluta. Il calcio ha da tempo imparato ad attirare campioni provenienti da altri universi sportivi. Steve Nash, leggenda del basket NBA, è entrato per esempio nel gruppo proprietario dei Vancouver Whitecaps nel 2008. LeBron James ha invece scelto il Liverpool, acquistando nel 2011 una quota di minoranza poi confluita nella sua partecipazione al gruppo Fenway Sports. E ancora... Nel 2023 è arrivato Tom Brady: sette Super Bowl conquistati e un nuovo ruolo da azionista di minoranza del Birmingham City, oltre alla presidenza dell'Advisory Board. Nello stesso anno anche JJ Watt, ex stella del football americano, ha deciso di investire nel Burnley insieme alla moglie Kealia.

C'è poi una storia che incrocia proprio i due mondi di Nadal: quella di Nasser Al-Khelaifi, ex tennista professionista, divenuto assai più celebre come presidente del Paris Saint-Germain. La sua parabola, iniziata sui campi da tennis e proseguita attraverso gli investimenti di Qatar Sports Investments, racconta quanto possano essere imprevedibili le strade che portano dal campo fino alla scrivania. Va detto però che Nadal seguirà un percorso differente: non entrerà nel Real Madrid con una quota azionaria, ma per portare il suo prestigio, il suo blasone e la sua esperienza direttamente nella stanza dei bottoni.

#2199. Birmingham City dodged bullet by not signing Middlesbrough man in the summer: Opinion

Birmingham City were said to be keen on landing the midfielder from Hadjuk Split in the last transfer window. However, the BCFC-linked man ended up joining fellow Championship side Middlesbrough instead.

Pukstas, 22, has since struggled for minutes at the Riverside Stadium and has played just 22 minutes for Kim Hellberg’s side since his switch to England. He is also currently out with a hamstring injury until early December which isn’t ideal.

Therefore, the Blues dodged a bullet by not signing the USA youth international and will be relieved that they didn’t fork out a hefty fee for his services. Boro paid £4.3million (via Transfermarkt) and handed him a long-term contract running until June 2031 but he has found it hard to get into their starting XI due to competition from the likes of Aidan Morris, Riley McGree and Sebastian Berhalter.

Birmingham City are sat in 3rd place in the table after 14 fixtures and slipped back into the play-offs after Wolves won 2-1 at Bristol City on Wednesday. They beat Millwall 3-0 at home last time out and take on Swansea City this weekend.

Birmingham City will have identified Pukstas as someone to bolster their midfield department. If he had joined BCFC, he would have given them more depth in the middle of the park.

At the age of 22, he has potential to grow and develop down the line. However, it wasn’t to be and the Blues were beaten to the punch by Middlesbrough.

Nevertheless, Chris Davies won’t be too disappointed now and he is well stocked up with options in Pukstas’ position. He has Paik Seung-ho, Tomoki Iwata, Jhon Solis and Taylor Gardner-Hickman at his disposal.

Max Bird joined instead of Pukstas in the end and he is slowly starting to prove his worth. The ex-Derby County and Bristol City man scored against Millwall earlier this week and is growing in confidence.

Birmingham City have enough options in midfield and also have Marc Leonard out injured. BCFC will get the chance to bolster their squad further in January.

The Blues are unlikely to look for another central midfielder this winter and will be happy with what they have. Instead, they are likely to look at attacking midfielders and wingers.

Davies’ men are on a roll right now and will be in good spirits. They are rock solid in defence and have the best defensive record in the division right now.

#2195. Rangers learn match officials for Saturday night Premiership clash vs St Mirren

The match officials have been confirmed for Rangers’ Scottish Premiership trip to St Mirren.

Rangers travel to The Marbill Coaches Stadium on Saturday, November 7, with the league clash scheduled for an 8pm kick-off.

Nick Walsh has been appointed as the man in the middle, with Frank Connor and Daniel McFarlane running the lines. Gary Hanvidge will be the fourth official, while Greg Aitken is on VAR duty with Peter Stuart assisting.

This won't be Walsh’s first involvement with Rangers this season. He took charge of the Premier Sports Cup clash between Rangers and Celtic at Ibrox in September, which Rangers won 3-0.

#2193. Wolves face spell without 22-year-old after midweek warning leads to injury

Wolves saw Jordan James forced off after 36 minutes in the 3-0 win over Cardiff City last Saturday, and he missed Wednesday’s victory at Bristol City as a result. The Express and Star has now confirmed the Welshman faces a short spell out with a hamstring injury.

James had complained of discomfort in his hamstring in the week leading up to the game, but he was cleared in scans and started. Further tests in the wake of his withdrawal ruled him out against the Robins, and the midfielder is set to sit out against Wrexham this Saturday too.

The 22-year-old, who had interest from Wrexham in the summer of 2025, will hope to return as soon as safely possible. James was not given a Wales call-up for the upcoming UEFA Nations League fixtures, so he will have the break to rest up and recover.

James has not played the starring role many backed him for at Wolves after his fine stint with Leicester City last season. He had to wait until October 13th for his first league start, but his back-to-back starting XI selections in the Watford and Cardiff City clashes suggested he was beginning to find his place.

In the midweek win over Bristol City, Jean-Ricner Bellegarde was brought into the side in attacking midfield. Marshall Munetsi, who James has also been battling with for a start, was in a deeper midfield role alongside Andre.

Bellegarde does have competition for his place though. Mateus Mane will hope to reclaim a starting berth having been benched for four of the last five matches, while Rodrigo Gomes could be reintroduced as well.

Tawanda Chirewa offers energy off the bench, so Cesar Peixoto is not short of options despite James’ injury.

As touched on before, James hasn’t made the impact quite hoped for at Molineux yet. He has had to battle for his starting spot and two goals in 13 games means he isn’t replicating the productive form he showed at Leicester City (34 games, 11 goals, four assists).

However, from his spell with the Foxes and in his Birmingham City breakthrough beforehand, there is no doubt that the Wales international can be a real standout at this level. He will hope to rediscover his previous best with the Old Gold yet.

Providing no further setbacks emerge, James can use the international break to recover and work closely with Peixoto and his coaches to help him have a bigger impact.

Wanderers face Charlton Athletic in the first game after the break on November 21st. It remains to be seen if the loan midfielder is fit and ready for the return to action.

#2190. Furious Redknapp demands ex Rangers transfer chief is axed for transfer blunders

Former Rangers technical director Nils Koppen should have followed Nuno Espirito Santo out the door, according to Harry Redknapp, who claims incoming West Ham boss Scott Parker is inheriting the club's worst squad in more than 65 years.

The London club axed Nuno after a disappointing start to their Championship campaign, with Parker set to be unveiled as his successor on Wednesday. But Redknapp believes attention should also turn to former Rangers recruitment chief Koppen, who oversaw a major squad overhaul and is now facing questions over the quality of the players brought to the club.

And that includes Arne Engels, the former Celtic star was signed by the Hammers for £22m but has yet to fully convince.

Parker faces an immediate test with away trips to Cardiff City and Blackburn Rovers before the international break.

Read more: Rangers officials confirmed for Saturday night Premiership showdown

However, Redknapp reckons the former Fulham and Burnley boss is walking into an unenviable situation.

The 79-year-old, who joined West Ham as a 14-year-old before later managing the club, delivered a damning verdict on the current squad and insists those responsible for recruitment must be held accountable.

Speaking to talkSPORT, Redknapp said: "Scott's got a great track record of getting teams up from the Championship. He knows the club, he was a very, very good player for them and I took him from West Ham to Tottenham.

"He's a very astute manager and I think it's a great fit for West Ham.

"I think the first thing he needs to do, though, is have a look at this West Ham team. Honestly, looking back over all those years, this is probably the worst group of players I've seen at West Ham in that time.

"So he hasn't got a very easy job on his hands."

Redknapp then turned his attention to the club's transfer business.

"The manager gets the sack, but who is bringing the players in?" he said.

"I don't know Nuno, but one after another managers come in and go. If I owned that football club, I'd be looking at the people responsible for recruitment.

"Who brought these players in? Who spent all that money on bang-average players?

"Whoever is responsible for that should be gone with Nuno, in my opinion."

Redknapp believes Parker's chances of guiding West Ham back to the Premier League will hinge on improving recruitment.

"Recruitment is all-important," he added.

"You can only work with the tools you're given. Back in the day, managers signed their own players and if they were no good, you took the blame. If they were good, you took the credit.

"You need players who can play if you want to play good football. West Ham was called The Academy because they had incredible players.

"It's a fantastic football club that needs resurrecting. If Scott's the man, hopefully he can give them that lift. But I'd be looking very carefully at whoever is bringing the players in."

In vista della partita contro il Portogallo, Signori descrive sensazioni diverse da quelle vissute da calciatore: «È un debutto diverso, ma per certi versi molto più emozionante. Sono carico di entusiasmo, sento una grande responsabilità».

Alla proposta di Malagò, racconta, non ha mai pensato di rispondere negativamente. La considerava un’opportunità attesa a lungo e non avrebbe voluto rinunciarvi per orgoglio o per la rabbia legata a ciò che aveva subito: «Assolutamente mai! Era una grande opportunità, l'aspettavo da tanto».

Signori, intervistato da Il Resto del Carlino, vuole partire da determinazione, sacrificio e passione. Intende aiutare i giovani a comprendere la responsabilità di rappresentare la propria nazione e l’orgoglio di vestire l’azzurro. A suo giudizio, oggi i ragazzi sono abituati ad avere tutto, mentre la sua generazione doveva conquistarsi ogni cosa.

Alla domanda se l’azzurro abbia perso fascino dopo tre Mondiali mancati, risponde senza esitazioni: «No, l'azzurro non passa mai. Quella maglia ti dà una sensazione forte, unica». Ricorda la competizione per un posto ai suoi tempi, quando tra gli attaccanti c’erano anche Baggio, Zola, Mancini, Vialli e Casiraghi. Per questo ritiene che i giovani debbano riconoscere il privilegio di giocare per l’Italia e dimostrare di esserne all’altezza, nonostante le distrazioni esterne e una società orientata, secondo lui, al tutto e subito.

#2188. Sembrava pronto per la rinascita della Triestina, ma Allegretti sposta il progetto Club Milano

Nei mesi scorsi il suo nome era stato accostato alla Triestina, per la rinascita del club, ma alla fine gli alabardati hanno puntato su Gaetano Fontana. Riccardo Allegretti, però, non si è fermato, ed è stato da poco nominato nuovo trainer del Club Milano, formazione militante nel campionato di Serie D, Girone B. Di seguito, la nota:

"La società Club Milano SSD aRL comunica di aver affidato la guida tecnica della Prima Squadra a Riccardo Allegretti.

Nato a Milano nel 1978 ed ex centrocampista di Triestina, Como, Lecco ed Empoli tra le altre, Riccardo Allegretti ha collezionato 68 presenze in Serie A e 253 in Serie B, mettendo a segno 44 reti totali in carriera.

Una volta conclusa l’esperienza da giocatore, Riccardo Allegretti ha intrapreso la carriera da allenatore conseguendo la Licenza UEFA A. Dopo alcune parentesi tra FC Cologno e Primorje FC, tra il 2019 e il 2020 ha seduto sulla panchina del Monza U17 prima e Monza U19 poi.

Nel 2021 è approdato alla neonata FC Clivense, iscritta al campionato di Terza Categoria dopo l’esclusione del Chievo dai campionati professionistici. Con la società veronese ha scalato le varie categorie, arrivando a vincere un campionato di Eccellenza nel 2023. Tra il 2024 e il 2025 ha poi guidato il rinato Chievo Verona in Serie D.

Oggi, alle ore 14:30, Riccardo Allegretti dirigerà il suo primo allenamento al Pero Sporting Club. La società ringrazia il mister per aver accettato con entusiasmo l’incarico e gli dà ufficialmente il benvenuto".

#2187. Derby County must turn to 43-year-old if they sack John Eustace: Opinion

Derby County have won only one of their first 14 league fixtures and they are in the drop zone along with Preston North End and Burnley. DCFC are seven points from safety behind Watford and Charlton Athletic following their 4-0 loss at home to Norwich City last time out.

The Rams’ hierarchy have a big decision to make about the future of head coach Eustace. If they are to sack the ex-Birmingham City and Blackburn Rovers man, there is only one man for the job at Pride Park.

Step forward Rob Edwards, who has been available since his shock summer sacking by Wolves and he has been weighing up his options in the game since. He has been carefully waiting for the right opportunity to arise and hasn’t rushed back into work yet.

The Telford-born man is a vastly experienced coach in the EFL and may feel he has a point to prove after his surprise dismissal from Molineux in June. At the age of 43, he is still young for a manager and he has overseen over 250 games from the dugout so far in his career.

If Derby County cut ties with Eustace, there aren’t that many available options at the moment. It seems unlikely DCFC would get someone like Steven Gerrard, Sean Dyche or Steve Cooper.

Paul Heckingbottom, Gary Rowett, Matt Bloomfield or Liam Manning would all be underwhelming and it costs money to get someone already employed. Therefore, Edwards seems the most obvious candidate.

He knows the league well, understands what it takes to get out of it and is someone who could help transform the Rams’ fortunes. As well as Wolves, he has also managed the likes of Forest Green Rovers, Watford, Luton Town and Middlesbrough.

Edwards’ best spell came at Kenilworth Road and he guided the Hatters to promotion to the Premier League against the odds during his time in Bedfordshire. They beat Coventry City on penalties in the play-off final.

Derby County need to act fast if they are to land Edwards. The longer DCFC leave it, the further adrift they could be cut and the less attractive they would become for prospective Eustace replacements.

Edwards has been linked with a switch to Wrexham recently and was also in the frame for the recent Burnley post. If the Rams lose again on Friday night to Bolton Wanderers, it would be hard to see Eustace surviving.

#2186. Hearts learn match officials for FC Thun and Falkirk double-header

Officials have been confirmed for Heart of Midlothian's upcoming clashes with FC Thun and Falkirk.

Hearts travel to Switzerland to face Thun at the Visana Stadion in the Conference League before returning to Premiership action against Falkirk at Tynecastle on Sunday.

Cypriot referee Kyriakos Athanasiou will take charge of the European encounter. He will be assisted by Marios Kalogirou and Kyriakos Sokratous, with Chrysovalantis Theouli appointed as the fourth official. Marios Christoforou will be on VAR duty, assisted by Dimitris Solomou.

Thursday's game is set to be Athanasiou’s first appointment involving Hearts. His previous European record also includes Conference League matches.

Hearts will then turn their attention back to domestic action when Falkirk visit Tynecastle on Sunday, November 8, for a 3pm kick-off.

Matthew MacDermid has been appointed as referee, with Chris Rae and Colin Drummond running the lines. Sean Murdoch will be the fourth official, while Steven McLean is on VAR duty and Gary Hilland will assist him.

MacDermid has previous experience of Hearts matches. He took charge of their 2-2 Premiership draw away to St Mirren in October 2025 before refereeing their 3-1 home victory over Motherwell in April.

#2182. Sheffield United man to attract transfer ‘interest’ in January window, says reporter

Sheffield United could face a battle to keep hold of the England youth international in the upcoming January transfer window amid potential attention from other clubs. He is under contract at SUFC until June 2028 meaning he still has just under two years left to run down on his deal at Bramall Lane.

Peck, 22, has become a key player for the Blades in the Championship under the guidance of head coach Chris Wilder. He has made 16 appearances for the South Yorkshire outfit in all competitions this season, 14 of which have come in the league, and he has chipped in with four goals and three assists.

Oxley has shared this update on his situation, as per a report by BBC Sport: “Peck’s stock is high, and rising. He has become a regular in the England under-21 side, he’s one of the first names on the Blades team sheet, and his underlying shooting, passing, possession and defending statistics show he ranks as one of the Championship’s best players in his position.

“He was coveted by Premier League clubs last summer, and you’d imagine he will be the subject of interest once again in January, especially after United sold a trio of stars pre-season to help balance the books.”

Sheffield United won’t want to lose Peck anytime soon, especially halfway through this campaign. He is a valuable member of SUFC’s squad and has the capacity to grow and develop even further down the line.

West Ham were said to be keen on him in the summer after their relegation from the Premier League. However, nothing materialised in the end and he stayed put beyond the last deadline in September.

Peck, who is from Worthing, has enjoyed plenty of game time with the Blades over recent times. He battles it out with the likes of Kalvin Phillips, Oli Arblaster and Joe Rothwell, among others, for minutes in Wilder’s starting XI.

He joined Sheffield United as a youngster back in 2021 after rising up through the academy at Arsenal. He has since played over 100 matches and was also loaned out to Oldham Athletic back in 2022 to get some more experience under his belt down in League Two.

Sheffield United’s chances of retaining the services of Peck in the long run will likely depend on whether they can reach the top flight. If they go up this term, he would probably stick around at SUFC.

If they stay in the second tier though, it would be a surprise if he stayed beyond next year. The Blades need to take advantage of his ability whilst they can.

Wilder’s men have been in good form recently and are genuine contenders for the play-offs if they can stay consistent. They beat Preston North End 3-2 last time out on Tuesday night with Peck scoring a hat-trick at Deepdale against the Lilywhites.

Sheffield United are now unbeaten in their last four and they are starting to build a bit of momentum now as they aim to get into the top eight. They are sat in 9th position in the table and they are only a point behind West Ham in 8th ahead of their upcoming match against Charlton Athletic away.

Fabrizio Romano, giornalista ed esperto di mercato, ha commentato sul suo canale YouTube l’esito del match di Champions Porto-Napoli 4-1, approfondendo le situazioni legate al mercato: “Ci sono sconfitte e sconfitte. Ci sono sconfitte brutte, ci sono sconfitte pesanti, ci sono sconfitte più accettabili. Ma ci sono sconfitte che lasciano crepe, sconfitte diverse da tutte le altre, e quella di Oporto per il Napoli ha proprio quel sapore lì. Ha un sapore veramente amaro, e non soltanto dal punto di vista della prestazione. Il Napoli ancora una volta si sgretola, crolla e va incontro a una battuta d’arresto che lascia scorie pesanti tra i protagonisti. Poco prima, Matteo Politano ci aveva messo la faccia, dicendo senza filtri: “Dobbiamo solo scusarci e lavorare più duro. Sono arrabbiatissimo”. Un messaggio forte che evidentemente è stato ricevuto. Poco dopo, infatti, arriva Scott McTominay: “Sono arrabbiato, non c’è niente da sorridere, sono veramente troppo arrabbiato per riuscire a descriverlo”. la questione dei contratti, un tema molto delicato in questo momento. Il Napoli sta lavorando da tempo al rinnovo di Scott McTominay, con grande fiducia tra settembre e ottobre. un passaggio fondamentale, perché il presente scotta, ma la programmazione passa da decisioni di questo calibro. Dall’altro lato c’è chi vede già la porta d’uscita. Prendiamo il caso di Frank Zambo Anguissa, che ha vissuto un inizio di stagione altalenante. La società quest’estate ha deciso di trattenerlo, ma con il contratto in scadenza, l’addio a parametro zero nell’estate del 2027 rappresenta oggi un’ipotesi molto concreta da monitorare attentamente”.

#2177. Blackburn Rovers vs West Ham predictions: Scott Parker backed for first win in ‘entertaining contest’

Blackburn Rovers head into Saturday’s clash with West Ham sitting 14th in the Championship table. Tony Mowbray’s side have endured an inconsistent start to the campaign, collecting 18 points from 14 games, with five wins, three draws and six defeats leaving them just outside the Championship play-off picture.

Rovers have shown signs of their attacking potential, though, beating Wrexham 2-0 at Ewood Park two weeks ago. Andri Gudjohnsen scored both goals in that victory, taking his league tally to nine and accounting for almost half of Blackburn’s goals this season.

West Ham arrive currently 8th in the Championship with 22 points and have been among the more prolific sides in the league, averaging more than 2.5 goals per game. Scott Parker, appointed following Nuno Espirito Santo’s departure, will be looking for a response after a disappointing result last time out.

WHUFC went three goals down against Cardiff City before eventually rescuing a 3-3 draw. Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman were all on target in South Wales, but Parker will be concerned by his side’s defensive shortcomings as they prepare for another difficult away assignment.

“Blackburn Rovers have shown enough attacking quality to suggest they can cause West Ham problems, particularly with Gudjohnsen enjoying an excellent run in front of goal. The Icelandic striker has become an important figure for Mowbray, while Todd Cantwell’s creativity gives the hosts another route to hurting their opponents.

“However, West Ham possess considerably more individual quality and should have enough firepower to trouble Rovers’ defence. Jarrod Bowen has been particularly influential, registering seven goals and seven assists in 14 league appearances.

“The visitors will need to improve defensively after their collapse against Cardiff, but their attacking options should ultimately make the difference. Blackburn can contribute to an entertaining contest at Ewood Park, although I expect Parker’s side to come away with all three points.”

“Blackburn Rovers have had some tough games of late. They were narrowly beaten by a great West Brom side, thrashed by Swansea City, and then hard done by to lose to Stoke City after a very soft disallowed goal late on.

“I think Rovers are going to see form come and go this season, largely because their squad isn’t very deep and relies on the form and availability of key figures like Andri Gudjohnsen, Todd Cantwell and Sam Morsy. Despite this, they’re dangerous with their front-footed play under Tony Mowbray and have had some good results at home.

“West Ham will be one to keep an eye on this weekend though. They will have had a couple of days to work under Scott Parker on the training ground after that great comeback draw at Cardiff City.

“We’ll get a better idea of what to expect from WHUFC as they settle into Parker’s management. I do think they have the talent to win the midfield battle though, and with quality players like Jarrod Bowen, I can see them creating the chances to win this one.”

#2176. Ex-Celtic star suffers major injury scare just hours after Scotland call

Former Celtic defender Stephen Welsh has handed Scotland boss Sebastien Pocognoli an injury scare just hours after being named in his latest squad.

The Swansea City centre-back was forced off after only 24 minutes of his side’s clash away to Portsmouth following a late challenge from Ebou Adams.

Welsh needed assistance from two members of the Swansea medical team as he made his way off the pitch, raising concerns over his availability for Scotland’s upcoming Nations League double-header against North Macedonia and Switzerland.

The 26-year-old was included in Pocognoli’s squad earlier in the day, with the Scotland boss also welcoming several established names back into his group, including Scott McTominay.

#2173. Scotland on alert as Slovenia rocked by fan mutiny before crunch Nations League clash

Slovenia boss Bostjan Cesar has pleaded with their own fans to behave after a bizarre threat by Maribor supporters to disrupt their huge game with Switzerland in Scotland's Nations League group.

Scotland will be paying close attention to the other game in the group when Sebastien Pocognoli's side host North Macedonia a week on Friday, as they try and overhaul the Slovenians into second place in the section, after that damaging defeat at Hampden.

But the game between Slovenia and Switzerland is at the centre of a huge storm among the hosts.

Slovenia were originally due to play the game in Ljubljana, where they drew 0-0 with Scotland, but were forced to switch the game because of the poor state of the playing surface at the Stozice Stadium.

Read more: 'We must improve': Pocognoli spells out where Scotland have to up their game

They will now play for the first time in five years at the Ljudski vrt stadium in Maribor, but have run into a major obstacle.

NK Maribor's ultras - the Viole - have been engaged in a long-running dispute with the Slovenian FA following heavy disciplinary fines, protests and stadium bans.

And they have threatened to disrupt the game against the Swiss, which would lead to UEFA sanctions unless the Slovenian FA, the NSZ, close their South Stand section where they gather and leave it empty.

They unfurled a banner at the weekend which stated, 'NSZ the choice is yours: Close the stand or open your wallet.'

It provoked outrage in Slovenia and Cesar has urged fans to behave. He said: "I am a coach, so I cannot influence everything. But I believe that there will be no complications. I hope that real fans will come to support the national team, which has not played in Maribor for a long time.

"Let's make a positive story in Maribor and in the end we can all celebrate together.

"We have a great chance of getting second in the group, which was above our expectations at the start of the section."

And the Slovenian FA stated: "We hope and believe our national team will be welcome in Maribor and there will be no trouble."

#2172. ‘Catalyst’ victory can inspire Wrexham to a result in Wolves clash: Predictions

Wrexham beat league leaders Middlesbrough at home on Wednesday night. George Thomason and Ollie Rathbone scored two late goals for the Welsh outfit as they returned to winning ways in impressive fashion.

The Red Dragons are sat in 16th place in the Championship table after their first 14 fixtures and they have made a slow start to the season. They are only six points off the play-offs now behind West Ham in 8th position.

As for Wolves, they came from behind away at Bristol City last time out to win 2-1. Newcastle United loan man Sean Neave scored two second-half goals to earn WWFC an important three points at Ashton Gate against the Robins.

Cesar Peixoto‘s men have now risen into 2nd in the division behind Middlesbrough ahead of Birmingham City. The Midlands club were relegated from the Premier League earlier this year after they finished in the bottom three along with West Ham and Burnley.

“Wrexham’s win over Middlesbrough was much needed and has eased some pressure off Phil Parkinson. It has been a tough season for the Red Dragons so far and they will be eager to hit some form now.

“The Welsh side haven’t scored enough goals in this campaign and are lacking a cutting edge. In fact, only six teams have scored less than them in the Championship so far.

“Wolves found a way to win at Bristol City which is a good sign for a team eyeing automatic promotion. WWFC are now unbeaten in their last five games since their home defeat to Bolton Wanderers last month.

“Cesar Peixoto’s squad is littered with Premier League quality and they will fancy their chances in North Wales. However, I think it may end a high scoring draw with the hosts seemingly back on track now.”

“Wrexham produced arguably their best performance of the season to beat Middlesbrough on Wednesday night. That has to be a catalyst for Phil Parkinson if he’s to hold onto his job and if the Red Dragons are to push up the Championship table.

“Another tricky game awaits here with Wolves unbeaten in five, but another home match marks a chance for the North Welsh side to build some momentum and head into the break on a high.

“Wolves are in high spirits themselves though. They’ve responded to poor form with a five-game unbeaten run, the most recent being an impressive comeback win thanks to a Sean Neave brace. That will be a real confidence boost for the young striker as well, as he looks to step up to the senior game and deliver while Wanderers star Raul Jimenez is out.

“This is a tough one to call, as Wrexham are capable of picking up another win if they play like they did on Wednesday. However, I can’t see Wolves losing, so I’ll say this ends level.”

Le dichiarazioni del giornalista Giulio Cardone ai microfoni di Radiosei

Nell'intervento radiofonico di questa mattina il giornalista Giulio Cardone, giornalista de La Repubblica, ha commentato gli ultimi aggiornamenti in casa Lazio.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Tavaras è affaticato, però dovrebbe iniziare la gara per poi magare lasciare il campo a mezz’ora dalla fine.

La Lazio deve guardare con grande attenzione Napoli-Roma, lì loro hanno fatto una buona partita. Il Napoli domenica o crolla o si rilancia.

Cataldi e Rovella? Cataldi sta bene, non ha tutta la partita sulle gambe. Rovella andrà in panchina domenica, così come Marusic; sono stati recuperati, poi avranno tutta la sosta per lavorare bene. Gudmundsson dopo la pausa, da capire se già contro il Lecce.

Per quanto riguarda lo stadio Flaminio, io so che l’accordo è per una capienza di 47mila spettatori. La Lazio non era disposta a scendere sotto i 45mila. Ci sono altre situazioni da chiarire, ma al momento l’inter sta andando avanti. Parlano di incontri positivi, ma non credo arriveranno ulteriori risposte entro il 2026.

#2170. Milan e Ferencvaros ai poli opposti: Ramos da solo guadagna più di tutti gli ungheresi

Questa sera, a San Siro, Milan e Ferencvaros si affrontano nella quarta giornata della fase campionato di Europa League. La vigilia consegna un quadro inatteso: dopo tre turni, gli ungheresi sono a punteggio pieno, con nove punti, mentre i rossoneri ne hanno raccolti appena tre. Dopo le sconfitte contro Benfica e Bournemouth e il successo sul campo del Salisburgo, il Diavolo è chiamato così a reagire davanti al proprio pubblico per non perdere ulteriore terreno nella corsa alla qualificazione.

Il divario tra le due avversarie, almeno sulla carta, emerge in primis dai conti. L’ingaggio lordo annuo di Gonçalo Ramos, pari a 12,8 milioni di euro (circa sette netti, bonus compresi), supera infatti l’intero monte stipendi del Ferencvaros, stimato in 11 milioni totali. Il più pagato tra gli ungheresi è Naby Keita, ex Liverpool che cerca a Budapest il rilancio e guadagna poco più di 1,2 milioni lordi a stagione. Poco sotto c’è l'esterno serbo Veljko Birmancevic, ex Sparta Praga e Getafe, mentre il salario di Kristoffer Zachariassen si attesta intorno ai 700.000 euro. Il trequartista norvegese ha segnato peraltro tre reti in questa competizione, come il milanista Cisse.

Se il confronto economico è sbilanciato, quello sportivo lo è però decisamente meno. I bilanci delle due società evidenziano una differenza netta di dimensioni (e ambizioni), ma non segnano certo gol in campo: i rossoneri fra poche ore dovranno quindi evitare cali di concentrazione e trasformare il loro vantaggio tecnico e finanziario in un risultato concreto. A partire da Gonçalo Ramos, chiamato a incrementare il suo attuale bottino di sole tre reti in ben 12 presenze stagionali.

#2167. 45-cap Rangers star left out for Scotland clash as national boss reveals reason

Bojan Miovski has been ditched by North Macedonia for their Nations League game in Scotland after manager Goce Sedloski confirmed it was due to his lack of playing time at Rangers.

The 27-year-old has been a key man for his national team since making his debut five years ago and has won 45 caps.

However, he's been relegated to a bit-part player at Rangers under Derek McInnes and has played a total of just 72 minutes all season for the Ibrox club.

The former Girona hitman has made eight appearances off the bench, mostly for very short periods, although he did net a vital last-gasp winner against St Mirren in September for his only goal of the season.

But despite featuring for North Macedonia in all four Nations League games, including starting the 2-0 defeat to Scotland in Skopje, he wasn't included in their squad for their final two games... including one at Hampden.

Rangers learn match officials for Saturday night Premiership clash vs St Mirren

Rangers hero 'set' for new role at EFL side after leaving last club over summer

Why Sebastien Pocognoli picked James Penrice as he talks up Rangers star's quality

Sedloski said: "Miovski has not had playing time at Glasgow Rangers for a long time; that's why I decided on the four strikers I selected. He has not been deleted from the squad - when he has more playing time and when he plays, he will get a call-up.

"There is no anger about the decision. I have always decided to select those who are playing, and I will continue that way. Those who do not play in the teams will not be called up. Let's fix the mistakes we made here against Scotland.

"We are playing against a strong team like Scotland, and we need to forget about our recent form and give everything."

North Macedonia have not scored for eight games and failed to win a match in their last dozen games.

#2166. Verdict split in Watford vs Middlesbrough predictions as hosts show much-needed ‘grit’

Watford have endured a tough start to the Championship season under head coach Alessio Dionisi. They are positioned in 20th place in the table after 14 fixtures and are four points above the bottom three.

The Hornets won 1-0 at home against Lincoln City last time out which was a much needed victory. Summer signing Stephy Mavididi got their winner against the Imps in Hertfordshire.

Middlesbrough lost 2-0 away at Wrexham on Wednesday night and were without key striker Will Lankshear in North Wales due to injury. Boro missed his firepower in front of goal and slumped to a disappointing defeat at the Racecourse Ground.

Nevertheless, Boro remain top of the division as they eye promotion to the Premier League under manager Kim Hellberg in this campaign. They are one point above Wolves in 2nd and two ahead of Birmingham City, Stoke City and Swansea City.

“Watford’s win over Lincoln City last time out and eased some pressure off head coach Alessio Dionisi. They have now won their last two home games which should give them confidence ahead of this weekend.

“Despite their struggles, the Hornets do have some good individual players at their disposal like Stephy Mavididi, Iker Bravo and Othmane Maamma. They can cause problems for any opponents on their day when they are at it.

“Middlesbrough will be desperate to bounce back from their loss at Wrexham. They are a different team without Will Lankshear and if he is unavailable again on Saturday, they could be in trouble.

“Nevertheless, Boro are top for a reason and their defeat in Wales was their first one in the league since August in a run of spanning back 11 games. I think they may have to settle for a point in Hertfordshire though.”

“Watford have found a couple of important results in their last two home games. They haven’t been pretty but they’ve shown grit and got points on the board, which will matter in a battle against relegation to the Championship.

“This is a big task though, and Middlesbrough will be out for vengeance. Boro were really poor at Wrexham in the week, as they were far less efficient with their 62.2% possession than we have seen this campaign to date.

“When Kim Hellberg’s side last lost, they responded with a comprehensive 3-1 win over West Brom and went on an 11-game unbeaten run. They’ll be eager to bounce back in similar fashion, and I think they’ll be able to despite the Hornets’ improvements at home.

“I’m going for a comfortable away win as the visitors produce a strong response to their Wrexham disappointment.”

#2165. Dalla Lazio all'Ancona, passando per Empoli: Chiucchiuini da scoprire. Il talento che piace in B e C

Un campionato di Serie D che ha un tasso tecnico sempre più elevato, e che regala ogni anno diversi interessanti profili che vengono chiaramente poi monitorati dalle categorie superiori; profili tendenzialmente under, quelli al vaglio del "grande calcio", tra cui figura il centrocampista Lorenzo Chiucchiuini, intuizione estiva dell'Ancona.

Nato a Roma nel 2007, ha militato per 12 anni nelle giovanili della Lazio, prima di vivere poi un biennio a Empoli, società ben nota per il settore giovanile, reputato dagli azzurri tra i punti cardine del progetto. Società che difficilmente sbaglia, con gli under, e infatti non ha sbagliato nemmeno con Chiucchiuini, naturalmente "numero 10" (di piede sinistro) che può però giocare anche davanti alla difesa: calciatore agonisticamente dominante, dalle elevate capacità organiche, risulta già completo, anche grazie a quanto fa vedere in campo. Benissimo sull'intensità, eccellente nella capacità di lettura del gioco e nelle scelte, sa smarcarsi perfettamente dall'avversario alla ricerca della giusta soluzione. Ad Ancona sta già facendo la differenza.

Dopo una trattativa che la dirigenza dorica ha più volte definito complicata, il calciatore è arrivato nelle Marche, salutando l'Empoli e firmando con i biancorossi un accordo biennale. Osservatori di club di C e B lo stanno già osservando, anche "live", ma difficilmente l'Ancora se ne priverà nel mercato di gennaio, complice anche il fatto che il ragazzo si trova molto bene nel club, è totalmente focalizzato sul proprio percorso e sugli obiettivi della squadra: al momento, non ha assolutamente intenzione di cambiare lidi. In estate, poi, eventualmente, si valuterà. Anche se magari, un'eventuale promozione, potrebbe cambiare le carte in tavola.

#2164. Dave Challinor confirms Championship job desire and rival interest before Sheffield Wednesday swoop

Sheffield Wednesday officially announced Dave Challinor as their new boss earlier this week. The move marked the 51-year-old’s first job since leaving Stockport County in the summer, but his success with the Hatters meant SWFC weren’t alone in eyeing him.

Speaking to The Star, Challinor revealed other clubs wanted ‘conversations’, and his eyes were initially on a step up to the Championship before joining the Owls – a club with ambitions of returning to the second tier. He said: “Without going into too much detail, obviously people wanted to have conversations. I was in the fortunate position where I didn’t need to jump back in. I came out of a really good football club, and we had success, so I had the luxury of being able to be a little bit selective and wait.

“My natural progression would have been to try and get a Championship opportunity. That made the number of clubs I am appealing to was probably pretty small. The next opportunity either needed to give me that chance, or be one I could grow with. I certainly couldn’t have picked a bigger one in terms of this club and the amount of growth it’s got in it.”

Challinor was more than justified in initially eyeing a step up to the Championship after his Stockport County departure. He took the Hatters from the National League to the brink of promotion from League One, but there was a mutual agreement that it was a good time to part ways.

His back-to-back top six finishes in League One and promotion pedigree made him the ideal candidate for Sheffield Wednesday. And, if the Chester-born boss wasn’t going to land a Championship job, moving to a third-tier side with promotion ambitions made for the next best option.

Arguably though, the SWFC job is more exciting than many in the Championship. The ongoing rebuild under new ownership and desire to wake this sleeping giant makes the managerial post a highly attractive one, so Challinor is progressing in job stature despite staying in the same league.

Challinor is a coup for the Owls, and it’s an exciting step for him too. It’s a win-win appointment that benefits all parties, as long as results follow.

Challinor watched on from the stands as Sheffield Wednesday played out a 1-1 draw with Blackpool under Matt Hamshaw last week. The new boss has been able to get to work with his squad this week though, and the first game is just around the corner.

SWFC face League Two side Oldham Athletic in the FA Cup on Sunday. It marks a favourable start for Challinor, both in terms of the level of the opposition and the fact that there aren’t points at stake.

David Storch has been eager to stress that this appointment is part of a long-term rebuild, and success will not be defined by how this season goes. Nevertheless, Challinor will be determined to get Wednesday firing as soon as he can to strengthen their case for a spot in League One’s top six at the very least.

#2163. Your 'loud and crazy' supporters won't stop us winning at Celtic Park

Former Arsenal star Leandro Trossard has warned Celtic that Besiktas are coming to Parkhead determined to take three points.

The Belgian international believes his side can handle the famous Celtic Park atmosphere as they look to continue their impressive Europa League campaign. Trossard joined the Turkish giants from Arsenal in the summer and has set his sights on going deep into the competition.

Celtic are still searching for their first points of the league phase following a defeat to Celta Vigo last time out, while Besiktas have enjoyed a strong start.

And Trossard insists the visitors will not be intimidated by the Parkhead crowd. He said: “We want to get three points from the Celtic match. It will be very tough. They have loud and crazy fans, but if we can translate what we want onto the pitch, then we will win. [SunSport]

“Our fans should continue their support. It would be great if they increased this amazing backing. People don’t pay any attention to it, but the support from the fans is incredibly effective in difficult times. We have won matches with their support.

Ex-Celtic star suffers major injury scare just hours after Scotland call

Celtic vs Besiktas: TV channel, live stream and kick-off time

Homegrown Besiktas insider offers Celtic intel ahead of European showdown

“They can be sure that if they continue to get behind us, we will win even the toughest matches.”

Besiktas sit fourth in the Turkish league but have made an impressive start in Europe, collecting seven points from their opening three matches against Marseille, Hoffenheim and Crystal Palace.

Trossard believes there is even more to come as they target a place among the Europa League’s top eight. He added: “I’m very excited and we want to reach the top in Europe. The decisive factor is the draw and the team’s performance during these next few games.

“Our current goal is to be in the top eight. After that, we just need to see how things progress. Every player wants to win trophies and to be champions. They want to be at the top at the end of the league.

“We want to reach the best possible position in Europe and there’s also the Turkish Cup. Besiktas aims for trophies in every competition. We have got this potential and we can do it.

"There were matches where we could have played better, but there’s a new coach, a new team, and an adaptation process - I know we can do better things.”

#2161. Exclusive: Matt Jarvis backs ‘exceptional’ Scott Parker in verdict on West Ham’s Nuno sacking

West Ham parted ways with Nuno Espirito Santo on Tuesday, and swiftly named Scott Parker as his replacement. The new boss led WHUFC for Wednesday’s 3-3 draw at Cardiff City, and many are backing him for success.

Among the supporters of Parker is former Hammers winger Matt Jarvis, who believes inconsistency ultimately cost Nuno his job. Speaking to The72 via Midnite, Jarvis said: “The fans’ perspective flipped from last year’s misery to expecting to dominate this league and win promotion. Visiting the London Stadium in front of 55,000 to 60,000 supporters is every opposition team’s cup final, and adapting to that takes time.

“With Nuno, people forget he had been there for a significant period. It took him time to establish himself in the Premier League, then they had a good spell before fading towards the end. The board backed him in the transfer market after relegation, but results remained too inconsistent. The supporters couldn’t see a clear playing identity, and the home defeat to West Brom proved the final straw.”

Jarvis went on to talk up Parker’s chances of success as West Ham boss. He picked out his communication skills, experience and passion as key traits as he looks to take the Hammers back to the Premier League.

“People will debate whether the decision came too early or whether they should have waited for the international break, but the club felt a change was needed. They have four upcoming fixtures where they should realistically target three points from every game.

“Scott Parker has a fantastic Championship track record and an existing connection to the club.

“He’s an exceptional communicator, which is vital for translating training-ground instruction onto the pitch on matchdays. He’s passionate, knows what it takes to get out of this division, and his arrival should have an immediate impact.”

Parker has won three promotions from the Championship, achieving the feat at Fulham, Bournemouth and Burnley. In his most recent triumph, he led the Clarets to 2nd place in 2024/25 and broke the league record for the fewest goals conceded in an entire campaign at just 16.

The switch from Nuno to Parker has split some opinions. There are justified concerns that their play styles are too similar given the new boss’ recent teams have been built on the foundation of a strong defence.

This was the case in his last job at Burnley, but the 46-year-old has shown he has the ability to switch up his approach. At Fulham in the 2020/21 season, his side had the second-highest average possession in the Championship with 61.4% (FotMob), as well 85 big chances created and 1,101 touches in the opposition box.

Over time, it will become clear whether Parker plans on emulating his Burnley tactics at WHUFC, or his Fulham style. Alternatively, he could switch things up completely, and if he does, ex-Hammer Jarvis believes he has the know-how and communication skills to get this across to his new squad.

After taking charge of the Cardiff City draw a matter of hours after his appointment on Wednesday, Parker leads his side for the second time against Blackburn Rovers this Saturday.

Midnite, the Principal Partner of Wolverhampton Wanderers for the 2026/27 season, are providing luxury coach travel on the “Midnite Express” for 100 Wolverhampton Wanderers supporters, as they set off on a 130-mile-round trip to watch their beloved Wolves take on the league’s glamour club, Wrexham, this Saturday, November 7.

Fans will be joined on the Express by Wolves club legends, Steve Bull, Carl Ikeme and Matt Jarvis and treated to club-centric quizzes and live Q&As, with exclusive prizes up for grabs.
