#2160. Julien Stephan's major QPR, Richard Kone boost confirmed pre-Lincoln City

QPR boss Julien Stephan has received a new boost following his side's frustrating 1-1 draw with Burnley involving the fitness of Richard Kone, who was initially hauled off against the Clarets.

The Hoops would fail to return to winning ways at home to Burnley after Saturday's bruising 4-1 away defeat in Southampton over the weekend, with the two results now leaving Stephan's men positioned in 11th.

14 games have now been played and QPR, for the large part, have appeared capable of challenging for a play-off spot, albeit contending with both the inconsistent form and difficult injury luck for star performers that has plagued the West Londoners in recent campaigns.

Much to the relief of QPR, though, one injury boost has now emerged in preparation for their return to action against Lincoln City at Loftus Road this weekend, swiftly allaying fears among the club's fanbase.

History looked to be repeating itself during the second-half of QPR's draw with the Clarets when Kone, who has battled through some testing injury setbacks already this term, was forced off in visible discomfort just before the hour-mark before heading down the tunnel instead of joining his teammates on the substitutes bench.

The Ivorian forward had been moving awkwardly and, after picking up a string of injuries in both pre-season and the early stages of the campaign, it was feared that another spell on the sidelines was looming as Rayan Kolli came on in his absence.

However, a new update from the London Evening Standard has revealed a major boost for the Championship play-off aspirants by confirming that Kone made it through Burnley's visit unscathed and will be available to face off against Lincoln on Saturday afternoon.

Perhaps, then, it was merely a precaution from Stephan, and the Frenchman will know that he has to carefully manage Kone's fitness in order to keep QPR's leading goalscorer firing on all cylinders.

QPR will be keeping a close eye on the situation involving Kieran Morgan's Bradford City game time as January approaches

It will now only add to the Hoops' confidence of getting back on winning terms against a Lincoln side who have lost each of their last two encounters against Birmingham City and Watford and currently sit six points shy of their next opponents in 18th position.

Kone's availability for the visit of the Imps represents positive news for QPR, who will feel even more optimistic of their chances against last season's League One title winners with his firepower to call upon if he builds on his most recent performance.

The 23-year-old has contended with some difficult months in West London and endured a stop-start introduction to the campaign blighted by injury and fitness issues coupled with a loss of form, although he now appears to have rediscovered his goalscoring boots with devastating effect.

Kone finally opened his account for the season just two minutes into QPR's shock 5-0 rout of promotion hopefuls Swansea City towards the end of last month. By the fifteenth minute, of course, the ex-Wycombe Wanderers sensation had scored a history-making hattrick, and even set up Jimmy Dunne's second of the afternoon to wrap up an unprecedented five-star display at Loftus Road.

Either way, Kone's clean bill of health is a significant boost for a club often accustomed to dealing with unfortunate injury setbacks to key performers, and perhaps nobody in Stephan's camp is more important than the top-scoring forward.

He is, after all, his side's highest goalscorer with four strikes from nine appearances, six starts and just 471 minutes of Championship football, working out to 0.76 goals per 90 minutes at this moment in time.

He wasn't able to get himself into valuable shooting positions, leaving QPR supporters to slam the striker's positioning, movement and even fitness.

And one supporter was particularly vocal about his criticisms of Kone, slamming the ex-Wycombe starlet's movement, position and technique in a damning verdict.

All in all, it remains difficult to deny the importance of Kone's fitness being intact for QPR's next match at home to Lincoln, as his scoring rate in limited minutes shows just how vital he can be under Stephan when operating at full throttle.

"The rest is about today: we’ve bounced back now, and we have to ride this wave.”

#2158. West Ham are having the last laugh as big Millwall controversy emerges

It's rare to see players cross a fierce rival divide to play for both clubs. In Benicio Baker-Boaitey's situation, he never signed professional contracts for both West Ham United and Millwall, but that was his decision.

The 22-year-old is currently under contract at the Den, but came through in the academy of Dockers Derby rivals West Ham. He was a highly coveted star as a teenager, and rejected professional terms to sign for Porto in 2020.

Rejecting terms after coming through your academy can often lead to a fanbase souring on you from the off, and when he joined Millwall from Brighton and Hove Albion in the winter window back in 2025, some Lions fans revelled in the prospect of seeing him flourish with them, instead of at the London Stadium.

That's not been the case, though, and as controversy emerges, Hammers fans are probably happy they got rid of Baker-Boaitey when they did.

It's been nearly two years now since Baker-Boaitey signed for Millwall. He arrived less than a month after his 20th birthday and was an example of the new transfer strategy at the Den, signing younger, promising players to flip for profit.

The 22-year-old's time at the Den, however, has been nothing short of a disaster. He's yet to make his debut for the club, wasn't included in their official EFL squad list for the ongoing campaign, and has now taken to social media to air his displeasure over the whole situation.

Posting to his Instagram story, Baker-Boaitey said, "On my mumma, can't even train with the 21s," before adding more stories and even changing his profile picture to Alex Neil briefly. Now, it's completely blacked out.

Neil is highly regarded as one of the more demanding coaches in the EFL, incredibly strict and not someone who seems to be ready to compromise over much. This, ultimately, is going to lead to some decisions that some simply won't like, and you can't help but feel for the 22-year-old, who must be feeling pretty isolated at his current club.

Baker-Boaitey was so highly rated as a 16-year-old that, before deciding to move to Portuguese giants Porto after rejecting professional terms at West Ham, he was being courted by Bayern Munich.

Whenever any youngster has links with those types of clubs, you always worry that they'll go on to big things and create some real envy, as it could have happened to you.

Injuries have more than played their part. A loan spell at Port Vale during the 2024/25 campaign in League Two showed glimpses of quality, but he only played 353 league minutes before being sent back in January amidst troubles staying fit.

It was later that window that he was sold to Millwall, and he hasn't seen the pitch since.

For Millwall fans in 2025, there wasn't much of an opportunity to get bragging rights over West Ham fans.

The two hadn't shared a division since the 2011/12 campaign, and while they themselves were showing signs of improvement, they weren't exactly banging down the door of the Premier League just yet.

So, when the opportunity came to sign a young winger who had rejected the chance to go professional at the London Stadium and, less than five years later, saw his future at the Den, it would have been a big deal.

Now, though, they're regretting signing Baker-Boaitey at all, and those fans who would have used the 22-year-old's arrival as a way to try and get one over on West Ham fans must be feeling quite sheepish right now.

Right now, it doesn't look like Baker-Boaitey is allowed anywhere near Millwall training, regardless of which team it's with. It's an awkward situation to be a part of, and Lions fans will hope that it doesn't prove to be a distraction on the field for them.

It's been a tumultuous campaign so far for Alex Neil's side, summed up perfectly by them following up a 4-1 win over Derby County last Saturday by going 3-0 down within half an hour against Birmingham City on Tuesday night.

But they'll be able to have a chuckle at what's happening between Baker-Baoaitey and Alex Neil, and breathe a sigh of relief that their former academy graduate isn't displaying this attitude at their club.

#2157. We asked supercomputer to predict how long Dave Challinor lasts at Sheffield Wednesday

Sheffield Wednesday will be hoping they can leapfrog up the League One table now their new manager is in place.

After parting ways with Henrik Pedersen just over a week ago, the Owls have moved swiftly to bring Dave Challinor to Hillsborough, who comes off the back of a glittering spell at Stockport County, taking the Hatters from the National League into the third tier in just five seasons.

The 51-year-old will be keen to have further success in the Steel City, taking on massive expectations and pressure that comes with leading such a huge club in English football.

Wednesday currently sit ninth in the League One standings following an inconsistent opening 13 league games, and the immediate challenge for Challinor will be to get his side winning games on a regular basis - something expected of the club when they possess the likes of Barry Bannan at this level.

With such immediate pressure to perform and turn things around, Challinor’s time with the Owls could go one of two ways.

It could either be a long and prosperous partnership, or his tenure could go up in smoke within a few months.

So, with this in mind, FLW has asked AI to predict how long Challinor will last as Wednesday boss.

It had been reported previously by journalist Alan Nixon that one of the only jobs Dave Challinor would venture back down into League One for was at Sheffield Wednesday, with the club then moving quickly to secure his services.

Challinor was announced as the new boss on the 3rd November, penning a three-and-a-half-year deal through to the summer of 2030.

Ali Al-Hamadi has reflected on his start to life at Sheffield Wednesday on social media, with his team-mate Barry Bannan reacting to the post.

And speaking in his first interview as the new boss, he reiterated his desire to win and getting the club moving on an upwards trajectory again, insisting the Owls have an “amazing squad.”

Speaking to BBC Radio Sheffield, Challinor said: "The aim is to win. The aim is to move forward.

"It's an amazing squad. We're feeling a little bit of pain at the moment with injuries.

"This is a new group, there was 17 or 18 new players in the summer.

"You go through processes of how that works and comes together, and you throw in the adversity of not being able to name the same squad consecutively because of injuries and suspensions.

"We know we need our best players back and that will hopefully not be too far away."

Challinor’s first game as Wednesday boss is against fourth tier outfit Oldham Athletic in the first round of the FA Cup, while they host Plymouth Argyle in his opening league game in charge on Saturday 14th November.

When asking AI to give their prediction of how long Dave Challinor will be Sheffield Wednesday boss for, it has predicted that he will depart the club in the summer of 2029, meaning he will last around two years and eight months in the role.

AI believes one of the biggest reasons Challinor could enjoy a lengthy spell at Hillsborough is his relationship with sporting director Simon Wilson, who have worked together at Stockport County.

A previous working relationship means they already understand each other’s footballing philosophies and expectations, while the communication should be considerably easier to help sign the types of players Challinor wants to recruit in order to implement his ideas as quickly as possible.

Meanwhile, his impressive promotion-winning pedigree should buy him some considerable patience from those at the top of the club.

After ending Stockport’s lengthy exodus from the Football League, he then achieved promotion with the Hatters into League One, demonstrating his ability to deliver sustained progress, which in turn should give the supporters and hierarchy confidence that he is capable of delivering similar success in Yorkshire.

Wednesday fans, do you agree with the AI's prediction? If not, how long do you think Dave Challinor will last in the role? Comment below!

The third and final reason Challinor stays in the job for that length of time is the club's need for stability, particularly when Wednesday have had to keep chopping and changing managers in recent years.

Wednesday need to establish a clear footballing identity and long-term strategy, so keeping Challinor in the role who has had such rip-roaring success in the EFL can only bring about positive change.

However, AI has also detailed reasons why Challinor won’t last beyond the summer of 2029.

It has detailed how such big expectations at Hillsborough could eventually catch up with him.

AI notes that despite Challinor experiencing managing ambitious clubs, the scrutiny that comes with managing Wednesday is on another level.

If he can’t deliver progress that sees the Owls be more than just an established Championship outfit, then patience could eventually diminish, leading to Challinor’s exit.

Meanwhile, there is a fear that Challinor won’t get everything he wants in the squad when it comes to recruitment, and if there’s a period of time deeper into his tenure where results turn for the worse, then supporters may not be as forgiving as they would be in the early days of his stint and may begin to voice their dismay.

Overall, AI believes that Challinor will fall short in seeing out his three-and-a-half-year contract, predicting Wednesday will part ways with the 51-year-old in the summer of 2029 to move into a different direction.

Dave Challinor appeared to be the standout candidate to take over at Sheffield Wednesday, and most connected with the club will be delighted that he’s decided to take the job at Hillsborough.

His job at Stockport County was seriously impressive and cannot be understated, with Challinor winning 142 of his 263 games in charge at Edgeley Park and overseeing a fantastic rise from the National League to League One.

The Owls certainly have every chance of success under his guidance, with the 51-year-old having a proven track record of improving players and getting his teams consistently finishing towards the top end of a league table.

Paired with his strong relationship with Simon Wilson, it appears that everyone at the club is now on the same page, with Challinor having all the confidence that the club can help build a team that he wants to build and can get every drop out of.

Such a rising star in the managerial game, Wednesday could well have picked up a gem in Challinor, who has all the capabilities of turning the tide and getting Wednesday back fighting towards the automatic promotion places.

This next period is one all Wednesday fans should be looking forward to, with the club backed to start putting in consistent performances and earning positive results in the coming weeks and months.

#2155. Barry Bannan reacts as Davis Keillor-Dunn drops Sheffield Wednesday message

Davis Keillor-Dunn's opening goal for Sheffield Wednesday last Sunday against Blackpool was a big moment for both the Owls and the 29-year-old.

It marked the unofficial first goal of the Dave Challinor era. The 51-year-old may not have been in the dugout, but he was in attendance at Hillsborough, having agreed on Saturday evening to become the new Wednesday boss.

The goal was also the culmination of some long periods of first-half pressure and, without the red card to Sil Swinkels five minutes out from half-time, the Owls would likely have gone on to score a few more and return to winning ways after picking up just one point from their previous three.

In the end, that didn't happen. Blackpool got level late on through ex-Wednesday loanee Jerry Yates, and the points were shared. That didn't detract from the milestone Keillor-Dunn surpassed with his opening goal, though.

Keillor-Dunn's effort to open the scoring was his third league goal of the campaign, and the 100th league goal of his career.

The 29-year-old has netted at least 10 league goals in each of his last six seasons, but it has been since he joined Mansfield Town in January 2023 that he has begun to really become an established marksman, first in League Two and now at League One level.

He's now netted 39 times in just 97 third-tier outings, the majority of which came at Barnsley before his switch to Wrexham back in January. A real struggle to get into the side at the Racecourse Ground meant he was stuck on 97 career league goals for quite some time.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

But Keillor-Dunn has now surpassed the century, netting a brace against AFC Wimbledon and then the opening goal on Sunday. He took to Instagram to react to hitting that milestone, stating, "Proud to have scored my 100th league goal, and what a place to do it at Hillsborough. Hungrier than ever for more."

Numerous ex- and current teammates reacted to the post, giving supporting comments. Wednesday midfielder Barry Bannan was the pick of the bunch, stating, "Go on the boy 👏."

The likes of Liam Palmer, Harry Gray and Jamal Lowe all joined Bannan in praising Keillor-Dunn for crossing that 100-goal milestone in Wednesday colours. Evidently, they all have the same opinion as those in the stands: they want the 29-year-old to stay all season.

Keillor-Dunn's loan spell from Wrexham runs out in January, meaning that a decision will have to be made about his future come the turning of the calendar.

With the Red Dragons set to be without Sam Smith for nine months due to a knee injury, Phil Parkinson may opt to play Keillor-Dunn back at the Racecourse once his spell ends. Wednesday will hope that's not the case.

Do you think Wrexham may opt to keep Davis Keillor-Dunn after Sam Smith's injury? Let us know in the comments.

The versatile attacker practically wasted six months of his career after making the mid-season switch to Wrexham in January. After netting 16 times in all competitions for Barnsley during the first half of the campaign, he only played 89 minutes from January to May.

Now, he's clearly loving his football again, and the phrasing in his Instagram caption, "what a place to do it," shows that he feels happy at this historic club. Wednesday fans want him to see out the season, so do his teammates, and by the looks of things, so does he.

Challinor was in the stands observing and making notes during Wednesday's 1-1 draw with Blackpool, and you'd expect he had some good things to write about after Keillor-Dunn put the Owls ahead.

The ex-Stockport County boss knows exactly what the 29-year-old brings to the table, having battled against him both while he was at Mansfield in League Two and then Barnsley in League One.

Through AI, we'll rank the top 25 most intimidating football stadiums in British football, but which stadium comes out on top?

Keillor-Dunn has scored four times in seven appearances against Challinor's Stockport outfit, so the new Wednesday boss must be pretty happy to have him on his side, at least for the time being.

The fact that the former Barnsley man has played for Wrexham already this season makes January's decision a lot more straightforward. He won't be able to play for a third side, so it'll be either a return to the Racecourse or a full season at Hillsborough.

If it's the latter, you get the feeling that he'll be set for a starring role in Challinor's side as the Owls strive to get back into the Championship at the first time of asking.

#2154. Wow' - Wolves star Adam Armstrong drops reaction after big personal moment

Wolverhampton Wanderers striker Adam Armstrong has taken to social media after recording his 500th career appearance in his side's 2-1 victory over Bristol City at Ashton Gate on Wednesday night.

The experienced striker has played for a plethora of clubs in the Premier League, Championship and League One, but for now is fully focused on aiding Wolves' aim of an instant return to the top-flight under Cesar Peixoto.

Armstrong played 85 of the 90 minutes in the West Country before being replaced by Tawanda Chirewa, just three minutes after his initial effort, which was saved by Brad Collins, was turned home on the rebound by Newcastle United loanee, Sean Neave, who went on to net the winner in the final minute.

It was a joyous occasion for all associated with the Black Country outfit, as a fourth win in five matches saw them climb into the second tier's automatic promotion places, just one point behind leaders Middlesbrough ahead of Saturday's trip to Wrexham.

Armstrong signed for his current employers in January from Southampton in a deal reportedly worth £7m plus £2m in add-ons.

The experienced forward has made 15 of his 500 appearances in the early weeks of the current campaign, during which he has scored four times, and will be hoping it ends in similar fashion to the 2023/24 season when he tasted promotion under Russell Martin at St Mary's.

Taking to Instagram after noting his personal milestone, the North East native said: "Wow what a night and what a win!!

"Who would have thought, 500. To the fans, teammates, staff at all the clubs. You played a huge part in me getting to this milestone.

"To my family. Thanks for all the support along the way. You are what drives me each day. Next goal promotion and 600 games," he added.

Among those in the replies paying tribute to the longevity of the striker's career were Ben Brereton Diaz, Will Smallbone, Harry Wilson, Bradley Dack and his current Molineux teammates, Mateus Mane and Tommy Doyle.

The Newcastle United academy graduate has enjoyed a fine first 500 games, with 102 of those coming in the Premier League, albeit he would have ultimately liked to have seen a better return than eight goals and nine assists in the top-flight.

However, Armstrong's clinical ability in front of goal hasn't ever been disputed in the Championship for the likes of Wolves, Southampton and Blackburn Rovers in particular.

289 of his outings have come in the division which he currently plies his trade in, and so have 94 of his goals and 37 of his assists, none bigger than his winner for Saints in the 2024 play-off final against Leeds United at Wembley Stadium.

Armstrong has started in nine of his 12 Championship outings so far this season, but it's fair to say the striker hadn't had the greatest of starts to the new season until recently.

He has since scored two in Wolves' past three games whilst having a big role in Neave's initial equaliser midweek, and this comes after receiving plenty of backing from Peixoto to start firing plenty of goals in.

“It is important for his confidence but he is an experienced player and I don’t believe he was losing confidence," said the Portuguese.

With Armstrong setting his dream out in terms of promotion, he will hope to start his next century of games with a positive result in North Wales this weekend.

#2153. Supercomputer names the 3 EFL League One clubs that will get promoted

We're a quarter of the way through the EFL season, which gives us a good sample size to set more informed predictions about how the campaign will play out in all three divisions.

In League One, Huddersfield Town and Stockport County have been the two sides who have consistently impressed and deservedly led the way in the top two.

But below them, Luton Town have picked up a head of steam and some strong underlying numbers are starting to translate into results. They even took a lead at the Accu Stadium on Tuesday night against the league leaders, but were forced to settle for a draw.

Then, further down the table, Sheffield Wednesday have appointed Dave Challinor, and many believe that's the start of a resurgence at Hillsborough. Leicester City have the quality on the pitch and a system that's starting to settle under Russell Martin, and between them, the likes of Reading, Plymouth Argyle and Oxford United will be competitive, too.

So, it's all shaping up to be a very intriguing promotion race with a lot of sides who will believe they can go up, but who does AI think will be in the Championship next season? Football League World has taken a look to find out.

Winning the league, quite surprisingly in AI's eyes, are Oxford. The surprise mainly comes from the Yellows being nowhere near the top six in many critics' pre-season predictions.

There was logic to that reason, too, as Oxford weren't able to spend money and invest in the squad over the summer due to an embargo imposed on them after failing to comply with the EFL's SCMP regulations.

Football League World takes an in-depth look at the TEN most frustrating EFL Championship players right now

The Yellows were also going into the season with a novice head coach, too, after Matt Bloomfield was sacked in favour of ex-Arsenal and Wales midfielder Aaron Ramsey. Quite bluntly, no one really knew what to expect.

However, Ramsey has more than hit the ground running in the dugout, and Oxford's embargo, despite its frustrations, did mean that the vast majority of their squad that finished 22nd in the Championship and had a mid-table points tally under Bloomfield stuck around. The likes of Mark Harris, Michal Helik and Ciaron Brown have been great.

But also, Ramsey has done well to re-integrate some players many fans felt would never wear an Oxford shirt again. Siriki Dembele has been the main example of that, but Gatlin O'Donkor and Louie Sibley have contributed well, too.

There are questions about the overall depth at the Kassam. Teenagers Mo Missanga and Leo Snowdon are seeing more matchday squads than perhaps expected; albeit Missanga has been brilliant, but hopefully the embargo can be lifted in January, and Ramsey can bring in some reinforcements to help solidify what AI expects to be a title charge.

The current league leaders, Huddersfield, have been forecast by AI to slip from the summit but still secure automatic promotion.

That may be a disappointment right now, but given that the Terriers have failed to even finish in the top six in their two campaigns at third-tier level since their relegation from the Championship, it's a position they should more than take.

There's a clear argument that Huddersfield's squad is already Championship quality. It definitely is at the top end of the pitch. Joe Taylor and Derensili Sanches Fernandes already have 25 goals and assists between them through 14 games, which is quite frankly ridiculous.

But behind them, Cameron Humphreys has been one of the best midfielders in the division for a couple of years now, and it's mental that Huddersfield were able to keep him around for a second loan spell from Ipswich Town, and Archie Collins is more than thriving at a top-end League One club after leaving Peterborough United.

It says a lot that there are question marks over where Ryan Ledson fits in when he comes back from suspension, with the Terriers' club captain and someone with close to 200 Championship appearances patiently waiting on the sidelines.

And it's all being overseen by Martin Drury, an exciting, young manager whose attention to detail and demanding nature of a good performance can ruffle some feathers in the 'only results matter' camp, but it's more than working so far.

There may be a slight drop-off, but one that AI believes shouldn't be too detrimental to their promotion push.

After failing in the play-offs for the last two seasons, AI thinks that Stockport will finally get over the line and secure a second-tier return for the first time since the 2001/02 campaign.

There were some understandable concerns about the immediate future of the Hatters heading into this season, with influential CEO Simon Wilson and manager Dave Challinor moving on after the play-off final, but those have been dispelled very quickly.

There's an argument to be made that County are even better now under Jimmy McNulty than they were under Challinor, and that's no slight on the good work done at Edgeley Park over the previous five years.

They have so many different avenues of attack, whether that be patient, on the floor, working through the lines with Ollie Norwood and a reintegrated Lewis Bate in the pivots, or they can go long and hit Kyle Wootton, who remains one of the best target men strikers in the entire EFL, given how well he can hold the ball up and be a goal threat, too.

Do you agree with AI's promotion predictions in League One this season? Let us know in the comments.

Stockport can sometimes get caught out defensively. It took them a little while to get to grips with their new out-of-possession play at the start of the season, and Oxford did showcase how to beat their high line, once they finally stayed onside.

But any worries of them fading away after back-to-back third-placed finishes seem to have dissipated already, and AI feels that it could be third time's the charm in terms of them earning promotion, winning ahead of the likes of Plymouth, Reading, Sheffield Wednesday, Luton and Leicester.

#2152. Fleetwood 1-1 Arsenal (5-3 penalties): Gunners knocked out of Carabao Cup as Max Dowman is sent off

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal suffered a stunning Carabao Cup exit. They lost on penalties to League Two side Fleetwood Town 5-3 in the fourth round at Highbury Stadium. A 1-1 draw meant Arsenal had to go to penalties. Teenager Max Dowman was sent off five minutes from the end of the game.

Fleetwood, in 18th place in League Two, took an early lead, but Viktor Gyokeres scored his first goal of the season to bring them level. However, neither team could find a winning goal. Fleetwood held their composure to win the penalty shootout after Bruno Guimaraes missed his spot-kick. The game was also paused for two minutes due to a drone flying around the ground.

Despite a heavy rotation by Mikel Arteta, Kai Havertz, Gyokeres, Martin Zubimendi, Myles Lewis-Skelly and Jurrien Timber all started. Martin Odegaard and Ben White were brought on during the half-time break, followed by Guimaraes, Ezri Konsa and Eberechi Eze. But overall the Evening Standard said Arsenal put in an “incredibly bad performance” over the course of the 90 minutes.

Dowman, 16, had been among Arsenal’s better players, creating chances for Havertz and Timber with dangerous crosses. He was shown a straight red card after reacting angrily to a heavy challenge from Harrison Neal and kicking out at the defender, though the force was minimal. It was his second red card this month, after a dismissal for the England Under-19s.

As Dowman walked off, Arteta was seen leaning towards him and delivering furious words. Asked what he said, the Arsenal manager told BBC Radio 5 Live it was “something that we discussed already before the match”. Suggesting the teenager had been warned to expect rough treatment. Arteta said he had not seen the red card back and that the referee’s decision stood. Sky Sports pundit Paul Merson was critical of how the Arsenal boss reacted to Dowman.

Arteta congratulated Fleetwood Town on the atmosphere they created. He said he could not fault his players’ attitude and intensity, with 11 men or 10. “When we create the volume of shots and chances we did, you have to score three, four or five,” he said. He added that even with ten men Arsenal had three big chances.

He called the unpredictability of cup football part of English football’s history, saying Arsenal were “extremely sad” to be on the wrong side of it.

Several squad members were absent. Riccardo Calafiori was out, and Noni Madueke had an ankle problem. Declan Rice was substituted in the previous game because of a concern.

Arsenal’s Carabao Cup dream of a first League Cup win since 1993 is over. Attention now turns to a huge Premier League trip to Anfield to face Liverpool on Sunday. Fleetwood, meanwhile, can celebrate a famous giant-killing and wait for the quarter-final draw.

#2151. Celtic alarm bells ringing as 'never seen before' signs spark Martin O'Neill warning

Robert Snodgrass admits he is seeing worrying signs at Celtic that he has "never seen before" as the pressure cranks up on Martin O'Neill.

The Hoops boss is feeling the heat after managing just three wins in 10 games, and Snodgrass reckons there are reasons for concern.

But it's not only results causing alarm, with Snodgrass fearing Celtic's off-field turmoil has seeped into the dressing room and left O'Neill fighting to keep the wolves from the door.

Speaking to Europa League odds, Snodgrass said: "I'm seeing signs from Celtic that I've never seen before, not just performance-wise but with too much political chatter being made public. Celtic's strength has always been keeping things in-house, backing each other and driving forward together.

Read more: Your 'loud and crazy' supporters won't stop us winning at Celtic Park

"It feels like board-level issues have crept into the squad, making it acceptable, it seems, to speak freely about negative tactical or internal matters. I don't like seeing it. The job at Celtic isn't just winning matches, it's winning titles. That's the standard.

"The main thing is to stop the talking and deliver on the pitch. They have a big game against Besiktas where they need a solid performance.

"Martin O'Neill's teams over the years were always solid. They're still top of the league, which is positive, but the high standards they've set in recent years mean expectations are huge.

"When you set those standards, people come for you, so you have to keep the wolves away from the door. We thought they'd turned a corner by beating Hearts, but losing 2-0 to Falkirk brings the questions back.

"Growing up watching Celtic's success, you know that facing adversity can actually be the making of a team. People feel they've left the door open for Rangers and Hearts instead of driving forward and pulling away.

"Seeing board-level matters reach pitch level is never good because it creeps into the players' minds. It creates a real challenge, but the only way to get everyone off their backs is to stick together and put in winning performances on the pitch.

"Every game at Celtic comes with huge pressure. The demand in Glasgow for both clubs is winning. They're powerhouses who hate losing. There's a specific demand to break down low blocks domestically, which is a different challenge from Europe.

"Celtic taking their domestic approach into Europe has been difficult over the last few years. Playing three games in seven days comes fast, so you have to rotate and manage the squad effectively rather than getting bogged down in off-field noise.

"You see Martin O'Neill using his experience to keep the players united through defeats because the games come thick and fast. Europe has been a test, but domestically, winning trophies is the absolute expectation."

#2149. Arsenal Women vs Benfica: Champions League preview, team news and how to watch

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal Women face Benfica at Meadow Park tonight, and as they prepare to enter a tough clash in the UEFA Women’s Champions League, head coach Renée Slegers has called for ‘grit and fight’ from her players. The match kicks off at 8pm in Borehamwood and will be available to watch live on Disney+ for UK fans.

Arsenal come into the match on the back of a 2-1 north London derby win at Tottenham. The team sit fifth in the European league with four points, having won 1-0 at home against HB Koge and drawn 2-2 at Paris FC. Benfica are currently sitting in tenth place but will overtake them if they win.

Slegers said the main factor behind Arsenal’s derby win was intensity. “It was a really intense game. We had to fight for it. We scored first, and we came back, so that’s what makes me really proud. Now we have to make sure we follow that up,” she said at London Colney on Tuesday.

Kim Little returns to the squad. Slegers confirmed that Katie Reid and Michelle Agyemang will not be back in time, while Frida Maanum is progressing, but the game comes too soon.

Taylor Hinds was a player of the match contender at Tottenham, playing a full 90 minutes for only the second time this season. Alongside Steph Catley, she linked up well on the left. Slegers has not named the same starting XI in back-to-back games this season, though, and said Arsenal will need “sharpness, quality and injections” late on against Benfica.

The referee is Ivana Projkovska of North Macedonia, who was among the officials for the 2025 Champions League final, when Arsenal beat Barcelona 1-0 in Lisbon.

Benfica have won all four of their Campeonato Nacional Feminino games as they look to retain their title, conceding once, in Saturday’s 4-1 win over Sporting CP. In Europe, Ivan Baptista’s side won 2-1 at Juventus before losing 3-0 at home to Bayern Munich. They have made more interceptions than any other team in this season’s competition, with 33, while Arsenal have made only 13.

Slegers expects a physical, compact opponent, similar in some ways to the derby, though with some changes in personnel since last season, when Arsenal won 2-0 at the Estadio da Luz through Beth Mead and Alessia Russo. Baptista said being seen as favourites means little to him and that his team will cause Arsenal difficulties again.

The record favours the hosts. Arsenal have won 10 of their last 13 home league-phase games in the competition, including the last three, while Benfica have won only three of their last 15 away matches in the tournament.

Slegers admitted the domestic start has been tough but said it is too early to judge and that Arsenal are proud to be back on the Champions League stage. Last season they finished fifth in the league phase before reaching the semi-finals.

#2148. Middlesbrough fans think Kim Hellberg got one thing wrong v Wrexham

Middlesbrough fell to their second defeat of the Championship season on Wednesday night, as Wrexham bounced back from consecutive defeats to pick up a vital three points at the Racecourse Ground.

In a game with very few chances, Phil Parkinson's side broke the deadlock with 20 minutes to go through George Thomason, and made sure the points were safe after Ollie Rathbone scored a second six minutes from time.

It's the first time that Middlesbrough have failed to score all season. Kim Hellberg's side have been electric going forward, led primarily by Will Lankshear, and it wasn't a coincidence that his absence after suffering a muscle issue led to Boro being a little less dangerous in front of goal.

However, it wasn't just the fact that Middlesbrough lined up with the likes of Kyle Joseph, Tommy Conway and David Strelec as the central nine at times on Wednesday night which frustrated Boro fans, as for the first time in quite a while, Hellberg himself wasn't without criticism.

Kim Hellberg is one of the more tactically fluid head coaches in the Championship today. He's comfortable deploying a back four or a back three, and spoke in his press conference last week about being happy operating with two strikers.

Unfortunately, the switch to a back three in possession and a back five out of it against Wrexham on Wednesday night has drawn quite a bit of criticism from Boro fans.

With Hellberg's side having a chance to extend their lead atop the table to four points last night against a Wrexham side who have underwhelmed this season and had lost their last two, the supporters were disappointed to see a perceived 'negative' lineup.

"Far too negative against a bang average side Kim. I hope you've learned from this? You know the score mate, sort it out. UTB!🇦🇹" one fan said on X in response to the Swede's post-match interview.

"5 at the back? More defensive against Moore? A team sat in the bottom half of the league, shambles Kim," another said.

Do you think Kim Hellberg got it wrong against Wrexham on Wednesday? Let us know in the comments.

Other fans accused Hellberg of "inviting Wrexham to give us a game," while others expressed frustration at setting up in a five "against a poor team." "He has to take responsibility for the formation and selections!" was also among the comments.

"Lost for words Kim? Poor team selection and formation tonight. Never play with a back 3 again," was said bluntly. Evidently, the majority of Middlesbrough fans are hoping Hellberg reverts to a back four on Saturday at Watford.

Ultimately, while Wrexham haven't enjoyed the start to the season that many expected they would have, Phil Parkinson's side is still full of talented players who can hurt any team on a good day.

This one can be chalked up to a sub-par performance from a Middlesbrough side and a bit of a better one from a Wrexham perspective, and the fact that Boro are still sitting top of the division regardless, just shows how strong they have been to start the season.

No league champion goes the whole campaign without losing, and those at the Riverside will be hoping that this is just a mere bump in the road en route to a Premier League return.

Kim Hellberg's side are in action at Vicarage Road on Saturday as they take on Watford in the final game before the November international break.

Against Cardiff City last month, the league's top scorer started on the bench but had to be introduced at half-time with his team 2-0 down. Now, Boro have lost by that exact same scoreline with him missing from the squad.

As Hellberg stated after the defeat, losing your top scorer would be detrimental to any side in the division, but Middlesbrough have to find a way to take chances and be better without theirs.

If this muscle setback proves to be worse than feared, Boro fans won't have to worry about a switch to a back three in the near future.

#2145. Arsenal injury news: Rice, Madueke and Calafiori concerns ahead of Liverpool clash

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal injury news has been in the headlines ahead of their visit to Anfield. Mikel Arteta confirmed Declan Rice, Noni Madueke and Riccardo Calafiori were all sidelined for their Carabao Cup clash against Fleetwood Town on Tuesday.

The Premier League champions lost 5-3 on penalties following their 1-1 draw at Highbury Stadium. Arsenal will face Liverpool at 4.30pm on Sunday. They have a number of injury concerns, plus a suspended teenager to deal with.

When questioned if the three players had just been rested, Arteta replied that Calafiori was unavailable. Madueke had issues with his ankle and Rice was “feeling something on the previous day”, making visible the reason why he has been substituted.

Martin Zubimendi replaced Rice in Saturday’s game when he substituted the player in the 80th minute of the game versus Everton due to Rice grabbing his hamstring. Madueke played till the end of the game. However, he was not spotted in training Monday, and Arteta did not provide any estimation for his return.

Calafiori appears to be in a better place. After being described as having a ‘slight issue’ by Arteta following the Everton game. DaveOCKOP reports that the defender is expected to be available against Anfield.

Winger Christos Tzolis has also been ruled out for five games with a hamstring injury. We’re likely to get an update on how all three are feeling going into the game from Arteta’s pre-match press conference.

Bukayo Saka is still sidelined with a quadriceps injury picked up against Bayern Munich in the Champions League. Arteta said the injury will take “weeks” to recover from, as he needs another scan due to fluid around the area. William Saliba has not played this season due to a back issue and is unlikely to return until December.

Arsenal’s problems were deepened at Highbury. Aaron Loupalo-Bi put Fleetwood ahead in the 21st minute. Viktor Gyokeres equalised five minutes after the restart, shortly after captain Martin Odegaard came on at half-time. Max Dowman was then sent off in the 85th minute for kicking out at Fleetwood’s Harrison Neal.

The 16-year-old will serve a three-match ban, reportedly covering the trip to Liverpool, the home game against Hull and the visit to Newcastle.

Fleetwood scored all five penalties. Odegaard, Kai Havertz and Gyokeres converted for Arsenal. Goalkeeper Jay Lynch saved Bruno Guimaraes’ effort, and 19-year-old defender Conor Haughey scored the winner. It was reportedly the first time the reigning top-flight champions have lost a League Cup tie to a fourth-tier side. There’s 84 places separating the clubs.

Arteta said the result was not down to attitude. “I’m sad, we’re all sad,” he said.

#2144. Melvine Malard becomes Chelsea’s leading scorer as FOUR-goal start strengthens her claim to the No.9 role

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Melvine Malard’s Chelsea goals are beginning to provide a persuasive answer to the club’s centre-forward question.

Her opener in Sunday’s 3-0 victory over West Ham took the summer signing to four competitive goals, making her Chelsea’s leading scorer this season.

That total comprises two goals in Champions League qualifying and two in the Women’s Super League.

The circumstances make those contributions particularly useful. Across three scoring appearances, Malard has broken a deadlock, restored a lead and helped Chelsea put difficult matches beyond their opponents.

Three of those goals arrived while Chelsea were level or protecting a one-goal advantage.

That gives the early return significance beyond its size. Malard has repeatedly contributed while there was still a contest to resolve.

Sunday provided the latest example. Chelsea had plenty of possession but needed somebody to turn an opening into a lead.

Turn and finish 💥Melvine Malard finishes for @ChelseaFCW 🔵#BarclaysWSL pic.twitter.com/eicBNrYMYk

According to Chelsea’s official match report, the Blues reached the half-hour mark without a shot on target despite having 64 per cent possession.

Sjoeke Nüsken created the breakthrough by winning possession and finding Malard.

The striker received the ball with her back to goal, held her position, turned and finished into the bottom corner.

It was an important demonstration of what she can offer centrally. Chelsea could find her under pressure and still get a shot away, rather than needing to release her into open space.

Nüsken’s contribution deserves equal recognition. Her pressing created the opportunity that Malard converted.

The sequence offers Bompastor something useful to build on: winning possession higher up the pitch and finding a forward capable of finishing before the opposition recover.

Malard’s first three Chelsea goals came as a substitute.

Her two-goal competitive debut against Real Sociedad immediately demonstrated that capacity to change a match.

Nüsken released her for the goal that restored Chelsea’s lead at 3-2. Ellie Carpenter subsequently supplied her second, extending the advantage to three goals.

The official account of that European victory records two different routes to goal: a run beyond the defence and a finish from a delivery across the box.

Against Birmingham, her alertness produced another valuable contribution. Malard punished hesitation over a backpass to score Chelsea’s second, eight minutes after coming on, as detailed in the club’s match report.

Those appearances give Bompastor flexibility. Malard has already influenced matches when introduced against tiring opponents, while West Ham supplied a scoring return from the starting role.

Chelsea signed Malard from Manchester United on a four-year contract, bringing her back together with a coach who first identified her potential at 14.

Bompastor later coached her in Lyon’s senior side. Their relationship therefore predates this summer’s recruitment by years.

Speaking when Chelsea announced her signing, Malard said:

“My objective is to become better every single day and to win trophies.”

Her next task is sustaining this contribution across a longer run of matches.

Competition remains productive. Aggie Beever-Jones replaced her at West Ham and supplied Maika Hamano’s late goal, giving Bompastor another reason to value both forwards.

Malard’s strongest argument is the range of situations she has already influenced. She has attacked space, finished crosses, punished hesitation and scored with a defender close behind her.

Four goals offer an encouraging early return. The variety behind them gives Chelsea something substantial to develop.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2142. Arsenal Women vs Benfica: Alessia Russo Hat-Trick Fires Arsenal Women to 3-0 Champions League Win Over Benfica

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal Women vs Benfica: Arsenal Women continued their unbeaten start to the UEFA Women’s Champions League league phase with a convincing 3-0 victory over Benfica at Mangata Developments Stadium on Wednesday night.

Alessia Russo hit a hat trick to claim the spoils and become the top scorer in English Women’s Champions League history. The England international opened the scoring before the break and added two more after the interval.

Despite controlling much of the game Arsenal’s attacking play was stymied by the determined Benfica defence and the inspired Lena Pauels in goal. The German made some stunning saves before Russo finally found a way through.

Arsenal came out firing after their win in the North London derby. Olivia Smith put Pauels under pressure after just 65 seconds.

She made another save soon after as the hosts took control of the game and created a number of great chances.

Geraldine Reuteler made her first start for Arsenal and nearly picked out Smith with a dangerous delivery into the penalty area. Russo also saw an effort blocked following a well-worked set piece.

Georgia Stanway came closest to breaking the deadlock during the opening half-hour. The midfielder escaped her marker before sending a powerful shot towards the bottom corner, but Pauels reacted well to keep the score level.

The Benfica goalkeeper continued her impressive performance by denying Caitlin Foord from the resulting corner.

Benfica gradually grew into the contest and threatened through Froya Dorsin, who wasted an opportunity by firing over the crossbar. The visitors created further pressure before Arsenal regained control.

Russo sent an effort narrowly wide before Smith forced another save from Pauels.

However, Arsenal finally broke through five minutes before half-time. Foord supplied a clever pass into Russo, who placed a precise finish against the far post and into the net.

Arsenal kept their forward momentum going into the second half and nearly extended their lead.

Kyra Cooney-Cross found an excellent ball over the top for Foord but was thwarted by a vital challenge from Catarina Amado.

Foord continued to pose a threat and got on the end of a cross from Ona Batlle at the back post. She powered a header across the goal, but Benfica cleared it in time to stop Russo getting on the end of it.

Smith then tested Pauels again with a deflected effort, but the goalkeeper could not prevent Arsenal from scoring their second.

Russo rose to meet Steph Catley’s corner and headed home to extend the hosts’ lead. The goal was her 22nd in the competition, making her the leading English goalscorer in UEFA Women’s Champions League history.

Benfica responded with a dangerous effort from Andreia Norton, forcing Misa Rodriguez into a good save at her near post.

Arsenal quickly threatened on the counter-attack, with Reuteler rounding Pauels before Diana Gomes cleared her effort off the line.

Pauels kept the game alive for Benfica but made a fantastic save to keep out Smith’s powerful strike in the 15th minute.

But Arsenal finally won the game after Amado brought down Foord inside the penalty box.

Russo stepped up and confidently converted from 12 yards to complete her hat-trick and take her Champions League tally to 23 goals.

Foord thought she had added a fourth during stoppage time, but the officials ruled out her effort for offside.

The result means Arsenal remain undefeated in the league phase of the tournament and also highlights the importance of Russo to the club’s European campaign.

They return to Women’s Super League action on Sunday, November 1, when they play Aston Villa at Emirates Stadium before facing Liverpool. On November 12 they head to Spain to face defending European champions Barcelona.

#2141. Danny Drinkwater on Chelsea move he ‘could never turn down’

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Danny Drinkwater Chelsea story still carries a sting. Five years at Stamford Bridge gave him plenty of time to think about how it all turned out, and his own verdict is not a happy one.

Drinkwater opened up in a Truth or Padel appearance alongside David Ornstein. He said the move was one he could never have turned down, even with the benefit of hindsight. “No, I don’t regret moving. I regret, in hindsight, how it’s turned out. I think I could never turn it down,” he said.

Strip away the headlines and the numbers alone tell an uncomfortable story. Drinkwater spent five years on Chelsea’s books. In that time he made just 23 appearances, according to the original interview report.

Do the maths and it barely comes to five games a year. That is a thin return for a central midfielder signed to compete for a first-team place. It is also the kind of record that rarely sits well at a club of Chelsea’s size and ambition.

Chelsea’s best attacking spells over a similar stretch of seasons look far more productive, as our striker rankings show. Set next to those numbers, Drinkwater’s five quiet years stand out even more starkly.

Five years. Twenty three games. These numbers alone explains why this transfer still gets talked about by Drinkwater, long after the headlines faded.

The numbers only tell part of the story. A string of incidents turned a quiet, underwhelming spell into something far more pointed.

Drinkwater had a training-ground bust-up while out on loan. He was also involved in an on-pitch brawl with Chelsea’s own under-23s side. Flashpoints between a first-team player and a development squad are rare at any club, let alone one with Chelsea’s standards.

Taken alone, either incident might have passed as a bad day. Taken together, it became harder to see Drinkwater simply as an unlucky squad player who could not catch a break.

The most serious moment came during his time at Aston Villa. Drinkwater headbutted team-mate Jota and was fined two weeks’ wages for it. He says the consequences at Chelsea did not stop there.

“They didn’t formally ban me, but I got removed from the first team building after the incident at Villa,” Drinkwater said.

One off-field incident, at another club, pushed him out of the manager’s day-to-day plans back home. No formal punishment at Chelsea followed. The effect was much the same.

Chelsea fans saw a similar breakdown of trust play out when Graham Potter struggled to win over the dressing room during another difficult Chelsea transition. Trust can break down fast once results or behaviour turn against the person at the centre of it.

Drinkwater’s comments matter beyond his own career. They are a reminder that saying yes to a big move does not guarantee it will work once a player walks through the door.

That tension has not gone away. Chelsea’s current transfer business, covered in our transfer watch, shows the club still weighing big calls on players whose futures depend on more than form alone.

Squad management under Xabi Alonso, assessed in our bomb squad verdict, raises a familiar issue. Players who fall outside a manager’s plans rarely find a quick way back in, whether the exile is formal or not.

Drinkwater never found his way back into Chelsea’s plans after the Villa incident. His account of the Danny Drinkwater Chelsea years stands as a blunt warning. A move that looks impossible to refuse can still become one of the hardest spells of a career.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2139. Arsenal Max Dowman Red Card Appeal Faces Rejection, Says Dermot Gallagher

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Former Premier League official Dermot Gallagher has said he thinks Arsenal won’t be successful in their appeal against Max Dowman’s red card in their Carabao Cup loss to Fleetwood Town.

The 16-year-old Arsenal winger was shown a red card for violent conduct after lashing out at Harrison Neal after a strong challenge from the Fleetwood defender. The Gunners then lost the fourth-round tie on penalties.

Arsenal are appealing the sending off of Max Dowman. They need to have their paperwork in by 1pm today (Thursday). They will be hoping to get a decision before their Premier League game against Liverpool on Sunday.

But Gallagher does not think they will be successful in their appeal as he believes there was clear intent from Dowman.

“I think it was a red card for the actions of the teenager,” Gallagher said.

He also thinks the referee should have given Arsenal a free kick as well as booking Neal for his challenge on Dowman.

Gallagher told Sky Sports News that the attitude of Dowman could be an issue for Arsenal in their red card appeal.

“I think he went out of his way to kick his leg up. It makes it difficult to overturn the decision,” Gallagher said.

“He’s got more of an aggressive reaction than what Cole Palmer had recently with Mateus Fernandes.”

But Gallagher was critical of the challenge made by Neal on the Arsenal youngster. He said that while Fleetwood’s defender did win the ball back, he didn’t think the challenge was made with enough consideration for Dowman’s safety.

“I think he should have got a free-kick and a yellow card,” he added.

The debate leaves Arsenal facing a difficult decision ahead of their Premier League fixture against Liverpool. If the appeal fails, Dowman’s dismissal will stand.

Sky Sports pundit Paul Merson said he disagreed with Gallagher’s comments, saying it was a harsh red card.

“It was a classic old fashioned tackle. He (Neal) wins the ball and then follows through. I think the reaction from the players might have had something to do with the referee giving Dowman a red card.”

Merson also warned the young Gunner that he will always be a target for opposition players.

Dowman has had disciplinary problems in the past, being sent off whilst playing for England against the USA. Merson feels the youngster needs to learn to control his emotions and not get caught up in the taunts of opposition players.

The incident has also raised more questions over how Arsenal are developing one of their most promising young talents.

Arsenal manager Mikel Arteta also attracted criticism for his reaction to Dowman after the Carabao Cup defeat to Fleetwood Town.

As the teenager walked towards the tunnel, Arteta leaned towards him and spoke to him. Merson questioned the approach, arguing that the Arsenal manager should have offered reassurance to the 16-year-old instead.

Arteta declined to reveal exactly what he told Dowman, explaining only that they had discussed the matter before the match.

The Arsenal boss also said the team had prepared for the emotional challenges they expected to face during the tie.

Jobi McAnuff similarly questioned Arteta’s response, arguing that Dowman remains young and will inevitably make mistakes as he develops.

Arsenal must now wait for the outcome of their red card appeal. With Liverpool next on the fixture list, the decision could have important implications for Arteta’s selection plans.

#2138. Keira Walsh adds another Chelsea weapon as former Arsenal target scores THIRD goal of the season

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Keira Walsh is giving Chelsea opponents another decision to make: protect the penalty area or step out to stop her shooting.

Her goal in Sunday’s 3-0 victory at West Ham took her to three for the season across all competitions. Each has come from outside the box.

For a player whose passing helps organise Chelsea’s attacks, that threat adds another way to influence matches. Recent performances show her contributing both to the construction of chances and their finish.

Against Sociedad, Walsh struck from distance after a corner, helping Chelsea complete their passage into the Champions League’s league phase.

The club’s account of that qualifying victory describes a tight encounter in which clear opportunities were limited.

At Manchester United, Lucy Bronze found her near the edge of the box and Walsh drove her finish into the bottom corner.

She also supplied Lauren James’ goal that afternoon, as Chelsea’s match report records. It was a useful illustration of the choices she offers: attempt the finish herself or release somebody closer to goal.

West Ham provided another example of that balance.

Walsh had already struck the post before Alyssa Thompson carried the ball in from the left and found Wieke Kaptein. Kaptein moved it on to Walsh, whose finish gave Chelsea breathing space.

The official report also records an earlier Walsh run down the channel and delivery towards Sjoeke Nüsken.

Her involvement therefore extended beyond waiting for opportunities to shoot. She helped Chelsea approach the penalty area from different positions.

There was a similar reward against Arsenal a week earlier. Walsh’s saved effort fell to Thompson for the only goal, as covered in our report of Chelsea’s derby victory.

That sequence illustrates why a dangerous shot can matter even when the goalkeeper stops it. Chelsea had a runner ready to attack the rebound.

Our assessment is that Walsh’s shooting gives defenders an additional problem. Closing her down can open a passing route; allowing her time leaves the goalkeeper exposed to a player already finding her range.

Walsh has also explained why she feels comfortable alongside Potter.

Speaking after the Arsenal victory in an interview published by Chelsea, she said:

“Lexi is really intelligent. She makes me feel really comfortable when I’m playing next to her.”

Her wider assessment emphasised collective responsibility, with team-mates covering for each other and working to make Chelsea harder to beat.

That matters when assessing midfield influence. Walsh’s freedom to help an attack depends partly on the positioning and decisions of those around her.

Potter subsequently started alongside her in Chelsea’s confirmed team against Lyon, giving their partnership another demanding European assignment.

The encouraging feature is the trust Walsh describes. Bompastor has an experienced organiser working with a younger midfielder whose reading of the game she clearly values.

BBC Sport reported that Barcelona rejected Arsenal’s bid for Walsh in September 2024.

Chelsea completed her signing in January 2025 on a four-and-a-half-year contract. Her official club profile outlines the technical quality and ability to dictate tempo that underpinned that recruitment.

Those qualities remain central to her value. Three early goals strengthen the attacking contribution around them, without establishing that she has changed position or should abandon her deeper responsibilities.

For Bompastor, the opportunity is to preserve that balance: Walsh helping Chelsea progress through midfield, supporting those around her and recognising when the space opens for a finish.

#2137. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”

#2136. Michael Chopra drops Cardiff City exit verdict on ‘unbelievable’ star

Former Cardiff City cult hero Michael Chopra has issued a claim on the form of Jack Moylan, whose fine start to life at the Cardiff City Stadium continued with a first-half hat-trick in the 3-3 draw with West Ham United.

Much of the pre-match discourse in South Wales was on Scott Parker's first game in charge of the Hammers, but it seemed as though the Bluebirds were going to make it three successive losses for them through Moylan's individual brilliance.

The Republic of Ireland international opened the scoring with a stunning free-kick after 11 minutes, before netting twice more before the interval.

After drawing a foul from Morato, Moylan beat Mads Hermansen from the penalty spot before glancing home his hat-trick and second in as many minutes just six minutes before half-time.

Ultimately, West Ham staged a thrilling fightback and were level on 82 minutes through goals from Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman, but that took nothing away from the stunning form Moylan continues to demonstrate in South Wales.

Who are the most clutch players in the Championship that could whip in a last-minute set piece worldie?

Moylan arrived in the Welsh capital in August from fellow Championship newcomers, Lincoln City, despite only signing fresh terms at the ProAmpac Stadium six months ago.

After scoring 11 times in 31 League One appearances for last season's third tier winners, the attacking midfielder is already the joint-second-highest goal-getter in the Championship, with his treble against the Irons seeing him match Andri Gudjohnsen's tally of nine for Blackburn Rovers.

The six-time international has posted three further assists as Cardiff sit 18th on 15 points, and Chopra, who scored 63 times in 159 appearances, had this to say on the 25-year-old.

"Said it a couple months ago @JackMoylan8 is some player," the former striker posted on X. "A first half hat trick unbelievable.

"@CardiffCityFC going to have a fight to keep hold of him in January," he added.

Many of Barry-Murphy's squad have earned praise in recent times for the way in which they have performed, with some still in the early stages of their highly promising careers.

At 25, there is also more room for Moylan to enhance his potential, which has only grown through his recent performances, and unsurprisingly, many Cardiff fans share Chopra's sentiment.

"I'm worried like you Michael he has come in and really lifted the club up players and fans we have really got to try and hold on to him our season will have a real bad hit if he goes," said one Bluebirds supporter.

Another responded: "No it isn’t mate cmon ! Only been here a couple months! End of season maybe but not January!"

However, not all were of this opinion, with another social media comment reading: "January is going to be a chance to see how the board see us when the bids for Moylan come in."

"Difference between Cardiff pushing for top half and being dragged into a relegation battle. Nobody else in that team has that level of quality in front of goal," was another verdict of Moylan's immediate impact.

Given Chopra's cult hero status at Cardiff, it was high praise that one Cardiff fan wrote: "Nearly, but not quite, as good as you!," in response to the former striker's opinion.

Cardiff fans, do you fear a sudden January exit for Jack Moylan? Comment below!

Moylan's performances have also been noted by supporters of other clubs, with a Birmingham City fan adding: "No chance he doesn’t get a prem move in January, bloke is class."

Few would be surprised if Moylan's current form perks the interest of clubs ahead of the winter window, although Cardiff fans will be hoping they avoid a quick sale of the playmaker.

However, the Bluebirds would still hold significant power in negotiations after agreeing a four-year deal with the Dubliner, whilst those associated with Lincoln are hoping that a sell-on clause was inserted in his sale.

At present, Moylan is proving to be the difference-maker for Barry-Murphy's side, and many will be expecting him to feature again in Heimir Hallgrímsson's Ireland plans against Austria and Kosovo next week.

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal face a major test of their Premier League title credentials when they travel to Liverpool on Sunday, with Mikel Arteta waiting on several key players ahead of the Anfield clash.

The Gunners will be looking to bounce back after a shock defeat in the Carabao Cup midweek against Fleetwood Town. However, injuries have hampered their preparations ahead of the big clash with four players injured.

Noni Madueke, Declan Rice, Riccardo Calafiori and Christos Tzolis are all in doubt for the game against Liverpool and could affect Arteta’s decision when selecting his team.

Saka will miss Sunday’s Premier League fixture because of a quad injury, leaving Arsenal without one of their most important attacking players.

The prospect of him returning is important as Max Dowman is suspended after receiving a red card against Fleetwood. Arsenal will appeal against it, but there is no guarantee that it will be decided by Sunday’s game.

Tzolis is also set to return soon. However, it is not clear whether he will be fit for the clash with Liverpool.

Havertz had a difficult evening; however, Arteta can still include the German in one of the most important fixtures of the season.

Rice will be another major talking point heading into Arsenal’s visit to Merseyside.

The midfielder was absent for the Fleetwood game after appearing to have hamstring issues in his appearance against Everton last weekend, but there is hope that he can be in the squad on Sunday.

It would give Arteta a real boost in the midfield going up against Liverpool.

Calafiori is also a doubt. He sustained an injury in the latter stages of Arsenal’s win against Everton. The extent of the injury has not been confirmed.

If the Italian fails to recover in time, then Myles Lewis-Skelly could be brought in to play as left back. However, the Gunners will have to see how Calafiori is before they decide what backline they’ll be using.

William Saliba is also out with an injury.

Arsenal are expected to make several changes to the side that lost to Fleetwood, but Madueke and Rice have to prove their fitness.

Predicted Arsenal XI: Raya; Timber, Konsa, Gabriel, Lewis-Skelly; Rice, Guimaraes; Madueke, Odegaard, Eze; Havertz.

Doubts: Riccardo Calafiori, Declan Rice, Noni Madueke, Christos Tzolis.

Arsenal visit Anfield on Sunday, November 1, 2026. The game kicks off at 8pm GMT.

The game will be shown live on Sky Sports in the UK.

Arteta will have some key decisions to make ahead of the game after Saka was ruled out and a number of players are fighting to be fit. Arsenal will be hoping to put their Carabao Cup disappointment behind them and put in a better performance.
