#2133. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2132. Real Madrid welcomes sporting royalty as tennis icon joins board of directors

Tennis star Rafael Nadal has become a director of Real Madrid.

The club’s board met on Wednesday and agreed to submit to the general assembly of delegate members ratification of Nadal’s appointment as a new member of the board.

Nadal won 22 grand slam singles titles in a stellar career, including a record 14 French Open crowns. He retired from the sport in 2024.

A club statement described Nadal as “one of the greatest legends in the history of sport and one of the most admired and recognised Spanish athletes in the world”.

The statement added: “For Real Madrid and for all Madridismo it is a great pride that a figure of his sporting and human dimension, a benchmark in Spanish and world sport, becomes part of our board of directors and contributes, from this responsibility, to the present and future of our club.”

Real’s statement continued: “With his extraordinary track record, with 92 individual titles, including 22 grand slams, two Olympic gold medals, five Davis Cups with Spain and number one in the world ranking, Rafael Nadal has starred in a legendary sporting career.

“But his figure represents much more than his successes: his talent, his spirit of permanent improvement, his capacity for sacrifice, his competitiveness, his humility, his respect for rivals and his team-mates, and his commitment to solidarity make him an example on and off the courts.

“All these values are part of Real Madrid’s identity, and Rafael Nadal has represented and defended them throughout his sporting life, becoming a reference for millions of people around the world.”

Nadal has spoken about his love of Real Madrid since childhood in previous interviews, despite his uncle Miguel Angel Nadal having played for their bitter rivals Barcelona.

Nadal has held honorary membership of Real for years, regularly attending matches at the Bernabeu. He was a guest of honour at the 2022 Champions League final in Paris when Real won their 14th Champions League title.

#2131. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

#2129. Max Dowman suspension leaves Arsenal facing another selection headache after appeal rejection

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

The Football Association has rejected Arsenal’s appeal against Max Dowman’s red card at Fleetwood Town, meaning the 16-year-old will serve a three-match suspension.

Dowman was sent off during Tuesday’s Carabao Cup defeat, leaving Mikel Arteta with another attacking selection headache. Arsenal are already without several wide players through injury.

In the 85th minute of the match, Dowman received a red card for responding to a heavy challenge by Fleetwood’s Harrison Neal. Although he did not make a full kicking motion, his foot made contact with Neal.

The decision was a costly one for Arsenal, who were already struggling in the game against their League Two opponents. They lost the game 1-1 and then saw Fleetwood win the penalty shootout 5-3.

They lodged an appeal on Wednesday but have now been turned down, meaning Dowman will miss the next three games.

Dowman’s absence adds to Arsenal’s current problems in the wide areas.

That is three injured players with Bukayo Saka, Noni Madueke and Christos Tzolis all sidelined. It means there aren’t many attacking options for Arteta to choose from.

But they have a big game coming up on Sunday when they go to Liverpool. Arteta will need to decide who he is going to play.

Dowman had another chance to shine in the game against Fleetwood. However, he only started for the second time this season. It was somewhat of a controversial night for the teenager.

This is the second time Dowman has received a red card in a short space of time having been sent off while playing for England Under-19s earlier this month.

The latest dismissal has also shone a light on how Arsenal deal with one of their brightest young stars.

As Dowman made his way to the touchline, Arteta looked frustrated. He gave a serious look to the young teen and appeared to say something in anger as he exited the pitch.

This suggests it was something that had been discussed prior to the game. Arteta later said that the coaching staff had spoken about the emotional demands of the game during their preparations.

The Arsenal manager said the players had received clear instructions about what they needed to respect and control emotionally.

The dismissal was due to violent conduct. The laws of football state that a red card can be shown if a player uses or attempts to use excessive force or brutality on an opponent, outside of a challenge for the ball.

This does not necessarily mean there has to be physical contact. In this case there was contact between Dowman and Neal.

The teen will miss the next three matches as a result of his ban. The focus will be on getting Arsenal ready for their Liverpool match with a lack of attacking options at their disposal.

For Dowman, it is important to avoid any further disciplinary issues.

He has made a few senior appearances already, thanks to his talent, but avoiding disqualification is also necessary for his growth as a player.

#2128. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2127. Spurs will be surprised but Leicester's won't by Oliver Skipp at Charlton

Having shone when coming through the academy at Tottenham Hotspur and then experienced a bright start to his career in the EFL, Oliver Skipp is again struggling this season, now at Charlton Athletic after a tough time of it at Leicester City.

Following their promotion back to the Championship via the 2024-25 League One play-offs, Charlton have looked to assert themselves as a part of the furniture again in the second-tier, under the management of Nathan Jones.

A young squad under Jones as they gained promotion, Charlton did have a fair bit of experience dotted around their squad but, as ever with a team heading into a campaign seeking to avoid ‘second season syndrome’, they have again sought to add more pedigree to their squad.

That may well have even been with a view to kicking on into the middle-of-the-table and, albeit as an outsider, looked at the top eight and the expanded play-off spots to eight teams in the Championship this season.

One player that was brought in with good pedigree to provide extra quality in the middle of the park was Oliver Skipp – but the 26-year-old is enduring another struggle this season down at The Valley.

Over the summer, Charlton added a fair bit of experience to the middle of the park with the signing of Nathaniel Chalobah on a free transfer from Sheffield Wednesday and Timothy Ouma from Slavia Prague.

Both defensive midfielders, it was somewhat surprising that The Addicks then signed Oliver Skipp on loan from Leicester City on the final day of the campaign – albeit in a Nathan Jones-coached side, perhaps you can’t have too many conservative and defensive-minded midfielders.

Skipp, Ouma and Chalobah joined a squad that already boasted other key midfielders such as Conor Coventry, Greg Docherty and the slightly more advanced Sonny Carey, while the versatile Joe Rankin-Costello is also capable of playing in a central midfield role.

Whether it be a 4-2-3-1 system or even a three-at-the-back with either one or two so-called ‘number tens’; there tends to only be room for two out and out box-to-box or defensive central midfielders – and with the quality Skipp has previously shown and the experience he already has in his mid-20’s; Skipp was viewed as a floor-raising addition to a Charlton side that had won their opening three games of the campaign before his arrival.

However, Charlton have since gone on a ten-match winless run of form after the arrival of Skipp and his own individual performance have perhaps been reflective of the malaise and regression of the team as a whole.

A look at seven clubs who should consider trying to sign former Tottenham Hotspur and England man Dele Alli.

The former England U21 international came into the side immediately and played the full 90 minutes as Charlton endured their first loss of the season – a 4-0 hammering at the hands of Stoke City.

He has gone on to start seven of the next eight matches, too, only not involved against Norwich City after being hooked at half-time in a 2-0 defeat to Watford a week earlier.

He came back into the eleven against Middlesbrough but found himself in a midfield that was completely overrun by the league leaders in a 3-0 defeat at the Riverside Stadium in that one.

Keeping out Coventry in midfield, as well as even Chalobah, who had started life in South London well, being moved to the periphery of things seems now to be a surprising inevitability for Skipp.

Once a metronomic figure in terms of his passing and tempo setting when coming through Tottenham youth teams and then on loan at Norwich; Skipp has badly struggled to adapt to the more conservative nature of Jones’ style.

Skipp has remained tidy enough on the ball with an 83.1% pass success rate but he is averaging too few passes per game and allowing games to pass him by, averaging more than 12 passes per 90 fewer than Conor Coventry, as an example.

Previously better known for his ability on the ball, it is his defensive work that has been his more impressive suit thus far this season but, with ball-winners such as Chalobah at the club, more quality was to be expected of the Leicester loanee.

That quality was shown when coming through at Spurs as he made the fringes of the first-team picture in North London and even began to emerge with more regular minutes when coached by Antonio Conte – who he once suggested he could help become a ‘top midfielder’.

His breakthrough into more regular minutes at Spurs had come after an extremely impressive loan stint at Norwich during the 2020-21 campaign – named in the PFA Team of the Year for the second division that season as The Canaries won the Championship title under Daniel Farke.

That season, Skipp was that metronome in Norwich’s proactive and entertaining side, averaging 51.5 passes per game with an 88% pass success rate – dwarfing his figures at Charlton so far this season and highlighting his drop off.

That drop off being seen by Charlton supporters this season may come as a surprise to Spurs and Norwich supporters but it won’t be much of a shock for Leicester fans.

Purchased by The Foxes in the summer of 2024 for a fee believed to be in the region of £20 million, following Leicester’s Championship title-winning campaign under Enzo Maresca, Skipp linked up with Steve Cooper at the King Power Stadium.

A good coach but a pragmatic one, the passing and ability to retain possession that had been qualities of Skipp weren’t necessarily going to be used too much for a Cooper-led relegation battler.

However, his fierceness in the tackle and general industry also diminished in a Leicester squad that succumbed to back-to-back relegations from the Premier League and into League One.

Perhaps a very good example of Leicester’s issues in the last half a decade, being a big money signing brought in without too much sense in terms of exactly what they wanted; Skipp badly struggled at the King Power Stadium.

Having been one of the best players in the Championship and then someone seemingly coming close to England recognition in the years before his arrival at Leicester; a loss of confidence and inability to rediscover his quality on the ball led to Skipp sticking out as a fairly hapless figure in Leicester’s badly underperforming side.

Brought in for such a big fee, he managed just ten starts in his debut campaign as they were relegated from the Premier League before being a peripheral figure in the opening few months of last season.

Skipp became a key man for a Leicester side that became enemies of the fanbase and his failure to rediscover any sort of form at Charlton thus far will not come as much of a surprise to Foxes supporters.

#2126. Chelsea’s £117m Morgan Rogers faced Bournemouth role problem as former scout explains why loan failed

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Morgan Rogers struggled to establish himself at Bournemouth because the role envisaged for him did not fit the team’s usual setup, according to former Cherries scout Des Taylor.

Chelsea’s £117million signing faces his former club on Saturday, with Taylor offering fresh insight into a difficult period in the attacker’s development.

Speaking to BBC Sport’s Nizaar Kinsella in a report syndicated by AOL, Taylor explained:

“We saw him as a number 10, but Parker was not really playing with a number 10.”

Rogers started only three matches and scored once during the loan under Scott Parker.

Taylor highlighted the combination of limited opportunities and appearances outside Rogers’ preferred position, while stressing that he did not blame Parker for the move failing to work.

It adds a specific explanation to the broader setbacks Rogers discussed in our earlier coverage of the experiences that shaped his Chelsea move.

Rogers approaches the reunion after scoring England’s seventh goal against Croatia, finishing after rounding the goalkeeper.

England face Czechia on Tuesday before Chelsea resume their Premier League campaign.

The Blues’ official weekly schedule confirms Bournemouth’s visit for Saturday at 3pm, giving Rogers the opportunity to face the club where his early progress stalled.

#2125. Arsenal transfer news: Bournemouth make Eli Junior Kroupi decision ahead of January window

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have been dealt a potential setback in their pursuit of Bournemouth forward Eli Junior Kroupi. The Cherries are reportedly refusing to consider a January sale.

The Gunners are not alone in keeping an eye on the 20-year-old after he returned from injury. Manchester United have also made enquiries, and Tottenham remains a strong possibility after chasing him over the summer.

However, Bournemouth are keen to keep Kroupi at the Vitality Stadium following the January transfer window. His return will provide new head coach Marco Rose with another attacking threat. Helping to improve their position in the Premier League.

This could mean that Arsenal will have to plan a long-term pursuit of the player.

Kroupi provided a positive impression in his debut season in English football. Becoming one of the most exciting young forwards in the Premier League.

Overall, he hit the target on 13 occasions in the previous league campaign. Attracting the attention of a number of top European clubs.

Paris Saint Germain, Barcelona, Chelsea and Tottenham were among the clubs that were said to keep following his progress over the summer months.

However, he was unable to make the move after suffering an injury to his foot which required surgery. Kroupi has now returned to action. He came on as a late substitute in Bournemouth’s 3-0 victory against Manchester United last weekend.

Arsenal have again been linked. The Gunners are now known to be one of a number of clubs to have asked about the player’s injury and fitness ahead of improving their attacking options.

Tottenham are also interested, having had previous interest in signing him. There is no real suggestion an offer has been made.

Bournemouth are the main problem for Arsenal, with Kroupa said to be unwilling to leave the Cherries in January. He is seen as an important asset for them in the second half of the season.

However, Rose’s side has had a difficult start to the season so far. Having him at his disposal could prove to be vital. The forward can bring goals and quality into the attack. Bournemouth believes it will help change their fortunes in the coming months.

They will also look to keep their attacking unit intact. Players such as Rayan and Marcus Tavernier are ones that Bournemouth will look to keep hold of. Alex Scott may also catch the eye of other clubs ahead of the New Year.

Bournemouth will be desperate to avoid losing a number of key players in one transfer window.

That stance leaves Arsenal facing a difficult decision if they want to pursue Kroupi. The Gunners can continue monitoring his performances and fitness, but a January move currently looks unlikely.

The forward’s return to regular football will give interested clubs another opportunity to assess his progress. Arsenal, Tottenham and Manchester United could therefore remain attentive to developments as the season continues.

Bournemouth are currently in the best position and Arsenal might have to wait until the summer to see how the Cherries will hold up when faced with losing one of their most promising young players.

#2124. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

Chelsea midfielder Reggie Watson scored while captaining England Under-17s on Sunday, although Germany’s late comeback ended his international window with a 3-2 defeat.

His England captaincy and goal add a concrete international development to that emerging club story.

#2122. Arsenal could expose Liverpool’s biggest weakness at Anfield

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have spent years struggling to impose themselves at Anfield. However, Mikel Arteta could have an opportunity to change that record when his side face Liverpool on Sunday.

The Gunners have often struggled against the physicality of Liverpool on Merseyside. Since Jurgen Klopp took charge, Liverpool have built a reputation for being high pressing, fast, and making their opponents make mistakes.

However, looking at the latest stats, it seems that Liverpool are no longer as physical, meaning that Arsenal could target their weaknesses.

The disparity is stark between the two teams, with Liverpool having run the least distance of any Premier League side this season. Meanwhile, Arsenal are running the most.

If Arteta’s side can capitalise on this and create fast attacks from possession, then they can utilise this to their advantage.

Liverpool have shifted away from their high-octane style associated with much of Klopp’s reign. Arne Slot has slowly moved towards a more possession-based approach, focusing on keeping the ball.

Andoni Iraola looks keen to add more intensity to their pressing. The Reds have had some of the best pressing stats in the Premier League this season (Opta Analyst).

However, they are not getting the desired intensity from the system.

In addition to chasing the opposition when they have the ball, players must quickly recover, cut off passing lanes and return to their defensive positions.

This is where Arsenal might have an opportunity. If they can get past Liverpool’s first line of pressure, they may have space to push forward before the home side can get back into position.

Another area that the Gunners could target is Liverpool’s midfield.

Alexis MacAllister and Ryan Gravenberch are technically gifted and very composed when they have the ball. But neither are a true defensive midfielder, which raises questions over their ability to stop teams playing through the middle.

Arsenal have the players to exploit this. Declan Rice can put in an energetic, physical performance and drive the play forward. Myles Lewis-Skelly provides another option who can run with the ball into dangerous areas.

This can cause problems for the Liverpool midfield. Arsenal can try to disrupt Mac Allister and Gravenberch, not letting them set the tempo of the game.

If they win these individual battles, it can stop Liverpool from getting the ball control they want.

The task is certainly a tall one. Arsenal have lost their last 16 games at Anfield, including seven in Arteta’s reign.

Whilst that may be the case, the current Liverpool team are very different to the one Arsenal have faced on a regular basis during Klopp’s time in charge.

They certainly can’t expect to be outplayed, but they must take advantage of Liverpool’s failings.

Arsenal are currently second in the Premier League with 21 points from eight games. They will want to win here but will need to do more than just run them into the ground.

If Rice and Lewis-Skelly can push forward and pressurise them, forcing them to make mistakes, then Arteta could finally have his winning recipe at Anfield.

#2121. Breaking: Man Utd begin formal progress for Michael Carrick replacement

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United are reportedly looking at options beyond Michael Carrick after the club believes he is not the right option for United.

Former Germany manager Julian Nagelsmann is among the names linked with a possible move to Old Trafford if United

Indykaila, a famous insider account on X, reported that Manchester United have started the process of finding their next head coach. Nagelsmann’s representatives are said to be willing to hear what the club has planned before deciding whether the project would suit the German manager.

Exclusive 💣Manchester United begin process to identify next head coach, sources indicate.Club sources have confirmed that Manchester United have started formal progress on appointing a new manager.Representatives of Julian Nagelsmann are open to listening to the club’s…

United have not announced any change in their managerial plans, so the report remains unconfirmed. Carrick is still in charge, but his results at the start of the new season have given the club plenty to think about.

Michael Carrick did enough during his interim spell last season to earn the job permanently. After taking over following Ruben Amorim’s departure in January, he won 11 of his 16 matches and guided United to third place in the Premier League, securing Champions League qualification.

That run convinced the club to hand him a two-year contract in May. The new campaign, though, has been a disappointing story so far. United have picked up just six points from their first six league games, their worst start since 1985/86.

Their latest setback came against Tottenham on Saturday. Bryan Mbeumo put United ahead, and Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card left Spurs with ten men, yet Dominic Solanke scored in stoppage time to make it 1-1.

Dropping points in those circumstances will only add to the pressure on Carrick, who needs a response from his players quickly.

Julian Nagelsmann has previously been linked with the Old Trafford job, and the latest report has brought his name back into the conversation. The 39-year-old’s representatives are reportedly open to hearing United’s plans, but there is no confirmed agreement or evidence that formal negotiations have taken place.

Carrick’s achievements last season bought him the permanent role, but the opening weeks of this campaign have left little room for further slip-ups.

#2120. Arsenal news: Arsenal vs Liverpool. Arteta targets Anfield record as Liverpool prepare for Premier League clash

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal vs Liverpool: Arsenal travel to Anfield on Sunday looking to maintain their strong start to the Premier League season and end a long wait for victory at Liverpool’s home ground.

A blistering start to the season has seen Mikel Arteta’s side win seven of their first eight league games, leaving them tied on points with Manchester City at the top of the table and six points ahead of Liverpool going into the game.

However, the Gunners have not beaten Liverpool in the Premier League at Anfield since 2012/13. Arteta will be aware that the Reds are starting to gel under Andoni Iraola.

They have won three of their last four league games after a lacklustre start and had their first league win at home this season with their 2-0 win over Brighton.

After replacing Arne Slot over the summer, Iraola brought his impressive three-year spell at Bournemouth to Anfield. However, Liverpool have struggled to adjust to the new manager, losing 3 of their first 4 league games.

But they have recently improved and are looking to play a much faster style of football. As Adrian Clarke notes, they are pressing more, not sitting back as much and wanting to attack quickly when they win the ball.

They have been the most direct attackers in the Premier League. They have pace to spare within their attack, with players like Alexander Isak and Cody Gakpo providing a threat in advanced areas.

Isak comes into the game in good form after finding the back of the net six times in his first eight league games. He has scored in three successive games in the Premier League for the first time since joining the club.

Florian Wirtz will also play a key role in Liverpool’s attacking play. The German has been consistently creating chances for the team playing behind Isak in a 4-2-3-1 system. He has created nine chances against Brentford.

Arsenal will need to limit the space available to Liverpool’s forward players. Allowing Isak and Gakpo to combine freely could make an already difficult away fixture even harder.

Arsenal have had some positive news on their squad ahead of their Merseyside trip. Declan Rice and Riccardo Calafiori are both back in training.

Christos Tzolis has also trained after missing October. Noni Madueke has also not been ruled out by Arteta ahead of Sunday’s match after he missed the Fleetwood game.

They also have their own fitness issues to contend with. Iraola confirmed that Dominik Szoboszlai and Victor Munoz are both available after suffering injuries earlier in the month.

Jeramie Frimpong will be missing due to a hamstring issue but should be back after the international break. Hugo Ekitike, Geovanni Leoni and Conor Bradley are all still sidelined with longer-term injuries.

The presence of Rice will be key for Arsenal. He has been one of the best progressive midfielders in the Premier League, along with their captain, Martin Odegaard.

Despite losing just one of their last eight Premier League clashes against Liverpool, Arsenal are not as strong on the road.

The Gunners have not won any league games in their last 13 trips to Anfield. They have drawn five and lost eight. Their last win was a 2-0 victory back in the 2012/13 season.

They have also never gone 14 games unbeaten away from home in the league. That would be a great achievement.

However, there is good reason to believe they can do it. They have only conceded five clean sheets in the league this season. They have faced the least number of shots on target and the fewest expected goals.

Liverpool have also been on a strong run defensively with four clean sheets in their last six league games, so Arsenal must make their chances count.

A win would provide another key result against a direct rival, improving their position at the top of the Premier League table and finally putting an end to one of the clubs’ longest-running away day woes.

#2119. Chelsea’s Romeo Lavia unlocks France with brilliant assist before Belgium suffer FOUR-goal late collapse

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Romeo Lavia produced a superb defence-splitting assist for Belgium against France on Monday, although a late collapse left the Chelsea midfielder facing a 4-1 defeat.

Lavia found Dodi Lukebakio with a forward pass from inside his own half, sending the attacker through to score in the 35th minute.

Sofascore’s match record credits Lavia with the assist, rewarding a moment of vision that briefly put Belgium on course for victory in Paris.

The Chelsea midfielder combined that attacking contribution with two interceptions and two tackles won during his 87 minutes.

Lavia started after suspensions ruled Kevin De Bruyne and Youri Tielemans out of Belgium’s midfield.

His assist demonstrated how quickly he can turn possession deep in midfield into a scoring opportunity with one forward pass.

However, Belgium could not protect their advantage. France’s late response left the visitors with a heavy defeat despite leading for much of the match.

Lavia now turns his attention back to Chelsea, who resume their Premier League campaign at home to Bournemouth on Saturday.

Arsenal will perform an MRI scan to monitor his progress. Arteta has suggested there have been positive signs, and he is expected back in December.

The main talking point will be Rice, Calafiori and Tzolis returning to training ahead of Sunday.

It would give Arsenal a nice boost with all three players fit. Madueke is still a doubt and they have fewer options going forward with Saka and Dowman out.

#2115. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

Fabrizio Romano has now confirmed that United consider the matter resolved for now and expect Martinez to remain available for selection.

“He spoke very strongly after the game against Atletico Madrid, which Man Utd lost. It was a very negative moment for the Red Devils. We know that Licha was very strong in his comments. He said that Michael Carrick was not backed enough during the summer transfer window.”

#2108. Arsenal news: Arteta’s side secure landmark victory at Anfield

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

In Arsenal news, Arsenal have finally found a way to win at Anfield again. Their latest victory owed as much to defensive discipline as Kai Havertz’s decisive goal.

The Gunners claimed a 1-0 Premier League victory against Liverpool, ending their 14-year wait for victory at the iconic stadium. Declan Rice set up their opener as Havertz put the visitors ahead on the 16th minute.

It was a tough afternoon for Mikel Arteta’s side as they were put under sustained pressure by their opponents. The Reds also created a number of dangerous chances throughout the match, especially in the first half.

Arsenal held their composure, and the backline was calm throughout. David Raya made the crucial interventions to keep the lead safe.

They are now three points ahead at the top of the Premier League after Manchester City lost to Brighton.

While the attack has been a key aspect of Arsenal’s title charge, they were put under pressure defensively at Anfield.

Liverpool came out aggressively after going behind. Virgil van Dijk caused problems from set pieces, and Florian Wirtz hit the crossbar from a free kick.

Raya was quick to block Van Dijk’s dangerous flick on and prevent Alexander Isak from capitalising. However, Alexis Mac Allister did head over the bar as Liverpool looked to level the game.

Arsenal were forced into another stretch as Jurrien Timber blocked well. William Saliba and Gabriel helped to limit Liverpool’s chances.

The stats tell the story of how well the visitors defended. Liverpool had 17 shots but couldn’t get one on target.

Liverpool were unable to create a shot on target for the first time in recorded history (since 2003-04) in a Premier League game at Anfield.

Meanwhile, it was the first time that Arsenal had kept a clean sheet in the Premier League at the ground since September 2012. The result was especially important following their Carabao Cup heartbreak against Fleetwood Town earlier in the week.

Whilst they have made a great defensive stand, Arsenal’s two most influential players in the final third made the difference.

Rice won the turnover, drove down the right and played a low cross into the six-yard box. Havertz made his run to the back post and finished to give Arsenal the early lead.

The goal also takes Havertz to 46 Premier League goals, making him the most prolific German in the competition and overtaking former Manchester City midfielder Ilkay Gündogan.

Rice had a huge influence on the game. His performance in the middle of the park helped Arsenal deal with the intensity of Liverpool, and he was named man of the match.

Christos Tzolis provided an important attacking outlet. His pace created chances to ease pressure on Arsenal. He forced an incredible save from Alisson with a late first-time effort.

The win was Arsenal’s first at Anfield in any competition since September 2012 and their 300th away win in the Premier League.

Arteta’s team have had a stellar start to the season, taking 24 points from nine games. They have also proved they are capable of getting results in tough circumstances.

They will now look ahead to their Champions League game on Wednesday against Slavia Prague and then back to domestic action against Hull City.

#2105. Arsenal injury news: Eberechi Eze concern emerges as Bukayo Saka awaits scan

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal came out on top in their match against Liverpool. Mikel Arteta admitted they have a new injury concern to deal with after Eberechi Eze was hurt. Bukayo Saka is also still out for the Gunners as they face two more games before the break.

The 1-0 victory over Liverpool at Anfield means they are three points clear at the top of the Premier League. Arteta will need to be careful with his squad ahead of their Champions League clash against Slavia Prague on Wednesday and home game against Hull City.

Eze was a surprise omission from the starting line-up, despite featuring well in recent games.

Arteta later revealed that the England international was suffering from a problem going into the game. He also praised the club’s medical and performance team for getting him fit enough to be included in the travelling party.

There is some light at the end of the tunnel, with Eze being named on the bench. There is uncertainty over whether he will be available for the trip to Prague.

Arsenal will look to train him to see if he can play against Slavia. However, Arteta’s comments suggest that the issue is significant enough that he couldn’t start against Anfield despite his recent form.

There is positive news on Declan Rice, Noni Madueke and Riccardo Calafiori. All three were able to overcome their fitness concerns to start the game against Liverpool and play the full 90 minutes.

The availability of these players provides Arteta with more options as he heads into a busy week. However, he will be keen to avoid any unnecessary risk to those that have only just returned to full fitness.

Saka is still the biggest injury concern for Arsenal as he has failed to play for the last three games after suffering a quad injury in the clash against Bayern Munich.

He was forced off after just 10 minutes of the Champions League draw. He will not be available for the fixtures against Slavia Prague and Hull.

There were concerns that Saka may miss up to eight weeks of action. Arteta hasn’t confirmed when he will be back on the pitch.

The Arsenal manager explained earlier that there was a need to scan Saka again to see how the fluid around the injury was behaving. The medical staff will also want to know if there has been any damage to the tendon.

This will give clearer information on when he will be able to return to play. Until then Arsenal will have to plan without one of their most important attacking players.

Another player looking to make his way back to fitness is William Saliba. The defender has not played this season after aggravating a back issue during his time playing for France at the World Cup.

However, he has avoided surgery and has been back in individual outdoor training at the club’s training ground recently. Arteta says there are positive signs that his recovery is going well.

The club plan for an MRI scan to see how he is recovering. They are hoping to get him back in December, but it has not been confirmed by Arsenal.

It would give Arteta a huge boost to his defensive options. For now he will have to keep monitoring his recovery.

They play Slavia Prague on Wednesday and then play Hull at home on Saturday. This gives them two more chances to build up their position ahead of the international break; however, Arteta will have to consider the latest medical reports.

#2102. Why this season feels so uncertain as a Newcastle United fan

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Hope you are all doing well, I am in my 40s and a long time Newcastle United fan living in Australia.

I eventually got the chance in September to come and see my first game at St James’ Park against Hull – it was brilliant.

I watch all the matches, read, listen to pods etc and have played all my life (I’m 42).

I’m also into the tactical side of things and have undertaken some of my coaching badges here in Australia.

I’m sharing my first submission to The Mag about why I think this season feels so uncertain as a Newcastle United fan.

Newcastle United are a club of ups and downs – we all know that. The club’s current reality feels like we’re on the way down, though in reality it is more like a straight line from last season than any sort of significant change :

2026/27 13th place in the Premier League after 9 games. 11 points in those 9 games at an average of 1.22 points per game. 2025/26 12th place in the Premier League after 38 games. 49 points in those 38 games at an average of 1.28 points per game.

There are lots of reasons that this season feels more ominous than last season, though primarily it’s because we have no clear idea what to expect from this manager and from this group and players. We don’t know what to expect.

For Newcastle United fans, this season is full of unknowns.

Was Eddie Howe keeping our heads above water?

Who on earth are half of these new players?

Could we… go down, or could we actually end up being alright with a few tweaks?

Never in recent years have Newcastle United fans had so much to be uncertain about.

Under the management of Eddie Howe, there was certainty. We knew him and he knew us. There was no chance we were getting relegated under Howe, even through last season’s struggles.

We knew he knew the league. We knew he knew how to win games in this league. We knew he knew the histories, we knew he knew just what a cold, wet night in Stoke meant. We knew he could (mostly) improve players on an individual basis and get the best out of them. We knew the players respected him, liked him and trusted him. We knew his tactics and his style, and we knew they worked (mostly).

We knew Howe’s team. We knew that when the chips were down, Bruno would take the lead. We knew he’d demand the ball, that he’d try with every last ounce of determination and skill to create something, to inspire his teammates and to inspire the fans. We knew big Joe had his back and would kick someone if he had to, or even if he didn’t. We knew we had a threat up front or out wide to hurt opponents. We knew we had guys who could score goals in the Premier League. We knew Tripps wouldn’t stand for a team that didn’t give their all. We knew we had high standards, kept by high quality players who had done it all and seen it all.

Now, all that safety and certainty is gone and you know what – it’s freaking us out. Without the guard-rails that the certainty of Howe provided for us, we’re contemplating the worst. We’re scared of free fall. We’re not cautiously optimistic – we’re venturing towards riotously pessimistic.

So, now, after a similar start to last season in terms of points, we’re worried. Not worried like we were last year – this time it’s different. We’re worried because we’ve got no idea what comes next. We’ve got no idea how Matthias Jaissle responds or what he might do to get a tune out of this team. We’ve got no real indication yet that this group of players can actually deliver at this level, under any manager.

We don’t know how PIF, David Hopkinson and Ross Wilson will respond to this, because they haven’t had to before. We don’t fully know whether they even know what they’re doing to be honest. They seem to for the most part, but here we are nine league games in, with fingernails dwindling, a hard run of games ahead and a bunch of youngsters or also rans to take us through to the derby and Christmas and the crazy winter schedule..,so maybe they don’t know what they’re doing?

So, while we gaze into the unknown, let’s also think about the knowns. We know we’ve got a great set of fans. We know that when St James’ Park is rocking we can get the team over the line against almost anyone. We know this year they might well need that and they’ll need us behind them to succeed in this league.

We know we’re in transition. We know we care. We know we want the team to succeed whoever the manager is, wherever he’s from and whoever is on the pitch. We know it’s going to be turbulent because when has it ever not been? And we also know, we don’t want to support anyone else.

If you would like to feature on The Mag, submit your article to contribute@themag.co.uk

#2100. Chelsea sweat on key stars as Bournemouth test looms

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Chelsea return to Premier League action this weekend with a growing treatment table and a lot of questions for Xabi Alonso to answer before Saturday’s visit of Bournemouth.

The main concern revolves around Joao Pedro. Chelsea’s current player of the season rescued a point against newly promoted Hull, but he suffered a knock and sat out the damaging 3-0 defeat at Brentford. The problem also prompted his removal from Brazil’s squad shortly after he had returned to it.

It is understood that he is undergoing specialist knee treatment in an attempt to expedite his recovery, although reports in Brazil suggest that he could be out for up to four weeks. No date has yet been set, but a return later in October looks most likely.

There is more good news elsewhere. Cole Palmer was among the players called up to the latest England squad, which did not go down too well with Thomas Tuchel.

The midfielder has been recovering from a persistent groin injury and has been working with a rehab coach. He is likely to be fit this weekend.

Moises Caicedo has also struggled with an injury this season, playing just 14 minutes across the campaign. He’s spent the majority of time on the bench recovering from a bad calf injury, which ruled him out for most of the season.

He made his only appearance as a substitute against his former club Brighton, but the game was red-flagged for Ecuador’s friendlies, so he did not play.

Alonso said before the Brentford match that the player would be ready to return. It seems to be the case, unless something went wrong.

Captain Reece James missed the Brentford game with yet another hamstring problem. Alonso said he only just missed out, so a return seems likely.

There may also be debuts. Marco Palestra, the £27m signing from Atalanta, has been sidelined since a training injury just before the opening match against Fulham. He is back in full training and was expected to feature in a behind-closed-doors friendly with Portsmouth. Chelsea lost 2-0. Emmanuel Emegha, injured after scoring against Bromley in July, is also pushing for involvement.

Less clear is the position of Jamie Gittens. He limped off with an ankle injury playing for England Under-21s against Kazakhstan and was seen on crutches. Mike Penders is fine after a quad scare on Belgium duty. Returning to the bench against Turkey.

Chelsea badly need a lift. Defeat at Brentford before the break left Alonso’s side looking shaky, and a squad stretched by injuries has not helped.

Saturday brings a chance to steady things. If Palmer, James and Caicedo are available, with Palestra and Emegha potentially adding fresh options, the picture will look much brighter at Stamford Bridge. Joao Pedro’s absence will still sting. However, a strong response against Bournemouth would go a long way towards easing the pressure and restoring belief.

#2099. Arsenal transfer news: Arteta considers January moves for two highly rated attackers

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal could have two ambitious attacking signings in mind for January. Eli Junior Kroupi and Kenan Yildiz are reportedly attracting Mikel Arteta’s attention.

Both players have emerged as potential targets as the Gunners consider further changes to their forward line. However, Arsenal may face a battle to prise either youngster away from their current club.

Kroupi is already familiar with the Premier League after making an impressive start to life at Bournemouth. The forward arrived from Lorient and scored 13 goals in 35 appearances across all competitions during his first season in England.

His development was halted by a broken foot in pre-season which required an operation to fix and kept him out of action for around three months.

The 20-year-old has now returned to the Bournemouth squad and has played as a substitute in their last three games. He will be hoping to get more game time as he gets back into full fitness.

Arsenal were linked with a move for Kroupi in the summer. His return to action has reignited interest in the player. A number of other European clubs also understood to be watching his situation.

Kroupi has attracted interest from a variety of top clubs including Manchester United, Tottenham, Bayern Munich and Paris Saint-Germain.

The French Under-21 international has impressed with his goal scoring in his first campaign in England. At only 20 years old, he has plenty of development to do under an experienced manager like Arteta.

However, Bournemouth are said to be reluctant to let Kroupi leave in January. Selling a bright young forward would leave Marco Rose with a tough decision to make.

It could get even more complicated if Alex Scott is also being seriously pursued. Chelsea and Liverpool are both linked with the midfielder.

Arsenal may therefore struggle to convince the Cherries to part with Kroupi during the winter window.

Another exciting option for Arsenal comes in the form of Juventus winger Yildiz. The winger has been linked with a move to North London for a number of transfer windows. He has once again been in the news recently at the end of the summer.

However, the Turkish international has struggled to get going on this campaign. He has only featured once in Serie A and has failed to feature since he suffered an injury.

Juventus have previously shown little interest in letting him leave. Head coach Luciano Spalletti firmly dismissed the prospect during the summer. Indicating that even an offer worth €100 million would not change the club’s position.

However, things could change next season. There are reports from Italy that Juventus may have to sell their star player if they fail to make the Champions League.

The Turin-based outfit are currently sitting in seventh spot in Serie A after just 10 games and are six points away from the qualifying spots.

This means that if they don’t make it into Europe, then it could put more pressure on Arsenal’s pursuit of Yildiz. It could create more pressure on them to raise funds.

Arsenal had a very busy summer with the addition of Bruno Guimaraes, Christos Tzolis, Ezri Konsa, Piero Hincapie and Illan Meslier.

But they also missed out on a few big names, such as Vinicius Jr and Julian Alvarez.

Despite this, there is still interest in Kroupi and Yildiz, suggesting that Arteta is still looking to strengthen his attacking options. Both players would bring quality and potential to the squad; however, there are some issues with both.

There are many unknowns surrounding this situation. Bournemouth aren’t keen to sell Kroupi and Juventus aren’t in a great financial position.

This doesn’t mean that either player will definitely be moving to the Emirates but much will depend on what happens at their clubs in the coming months.

#2098. Jamie Carragher names Man Utd Star who impressed him against Leeds

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United had a difficult evening once again as they were held to a 1-1 draw at Leeds United. Pressure mounts on Michael Carrick as United have just 7 points from 7 games.

Manchester United took the lead through Bryan Mbeumo in the first half, with the forward finishing Youri Tielemans’ excellent cross from the right. However, the visitors couldn’t hold on to their advantage as Dominic Calvert-Lewin equalised for Leeds United from a corner.

The draw extended United’s frustrating run of results, leaving Michael Carrick still searching for the consistency his side need.

Carragher, though, believes there were positives to take from the trip, particularly one performance at the back.

Jamie Carragher has singled out Harry Maguire for praise after Manchester United drew 1-1 with Leeds United at Elland Road on Sunday.

Speaking on Sky Sports, the former Liverpool defender admitted that picking up a point at Elland Road was not necessarily a bad result in isolation, even if United’s recent form made it feel like a missed opportunity.

“Going away to Leeds is going to be tricky for any team except for Manchester City and Arsenal at the moment,” Carragher said.

“A lot of teams will take a point at Leeds this season but the problem for Manchester United is the results have been so poor that they need to start winning games so it almost feels like two points dropped.”

Carragher then turned his attention to Maguire, who he felt played a major role in keeping United in the game when Leeds began to push for a winner.

“But in isolation, getting a point at Leeds is a decent result. I thought Harry Maguire was fantastic, especially in the second half.”

Leeds United grew into the contest after Calvert-Lewin brought them level, putting United under pressure during the closing stages. Carragher felt Maguire’s efforts in defence helped prevent the visitors from falling apart.

“After Leeds got that goal back there was a stage of the game where it felt like Man United could go under, but Maguire at the back held it together for them. He was fantastic.”

The result may feel like another opportunity missed for Carrick, but Maguire’s performance gave United something positive to take away from Elland Road.

#2097. Chelsea co-sporting director Laurence Stewart to leave in leadership reshuffle

Gary Gowers is a sports journalist and the editor of Read Everton, where he leads daily coverage of Everton news, transfers and analysis. He also writes about Manchester City for Read Man City and motorsport for Read Motorsport. A former accountant, he became a freelance writer in 2011 and has written for BBC Sport and Metro.

Laurence Stewart is set to leave Chelsea by mutual consent after almost four years at Stamford Bridge, with the club opting to redistribute his responsibilities rather than appoint a direct replacement.

The departure is understood to be amicable and won’t take immediate effect. Stewart could continue attending matches before formally leaving, following discussions with co-owner Behdad Eghbali over the club’s evolving sporting structure.

The story was first reported by Matt Law of The Telegraph and Nizaar Kinsella of BBC Sport, with Ben Jacobs subsequently providing further details about how Chelsea intend to operate.

Stewart arrived from Monaco in October 2022 as technical director before becoming co-sporting director alongside Paul Winstanley in February 2023.

Chelsea’s day-to-day sporting leadership will now be shared between Winstanley, Sam Jewell and Joe Shields, each with defined responsibilities.

Winstanley will continue overseeing transfer negotiations, player contracts and several departments at Cobham, including medical and performance operations. Shields’ work centres on the academy and player pathways, while Jewell has wider responsibilities across the BlueCo operation, including Strasbourg.

According to Jacobs, discussions about restructuring began in April and accelerated following the appointment of Xabi Alonso as head coach.

Stewart had overseen investment in Chelsea’s data analysis operation, although Jewell and Shields have increasingly taken responsibility for using that information in recruitment and talent identification.

Dave Fallows will remain involved in an advisory capacity, rather than taking on a fixed day-to-day portfolio.

The changes are being described as a natural evolution of Chelsea’s sporting model. There is no suggestion that Stewart’s departure followed a breakdown in relations, and the club are not currently planning to recruit a replacement.

#2096. Arsenal U19s face tough Slavia Prague test in UEFA Youth League

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal’s under-19s face a significant challenge when they travel to Czechia to take on Slavia Prague in the UEFA Youth League on Wednesday.

The young Gunners have picked up four points from their first three games and will be hoping to build on that in the league phase. Two wins would definitely help their chances of getting into the knockout stages.

They kicked off their campaign with a win against Napoli and put on a great show in a 3-3 draw against Bayern Munich last month.

Those results have left them 18th in the standings, 16 places below their next opponents. With the top 22 teams progressing to the knockout stages, another positive result could prove important for their qualification hopes.

However, Slavia Prague has made a much stronger start and will provide a difficult examination for David Horseman’s side.

Slavia have won all three of their first games in the UEFA Youth League and are sitting in second place. Just behind Real Madrid on goal difference.

They began well with a 2-1 win against French side Lens. They then went on to beat Sabah 3-0 and then put in another strong display against Fenerbahce.

Despite falling behind in Prague, the players fought back to win the game 3-1. The three wins means Slavia are already past the number of points they scored last season in the league stage.

They also advanced to the knockout stages last time, finishing 20th before they faced Benfica.

Mubarak Suleiman put the team ahead twice, but they were pegged back by two goals in the last 30 minutes as their European run came to an end.

This season they have looked like a much stronger team. With a perfect record, they will be one of the hardest teams that Arsenal will face.

The meeting on Wednesday provides an opportunity for Arsenal to get revenge for their heavy defeat in Prague last season.

The two sides met during the 2025/26 league campaign, with Slavia winning 5-1 at home.

Arsenal were three goals down at the break after an own goal plus goals from Bartosz Szywala and Jakub Kolisek.

O’Neill pulled one back after the break, but the home side had the final word as Suleiman and Matej Kotrba scored in stoppage time.

This loss shows how tough it will be for Horseman’s side. Slavia have already proven their attacking prowess against Arsenal and are playing even better in this campaign.

The young Gunners will have to play much more disciplined if they are to leave Prague with three points.

Arsenal fans can watch the UEFA Youth League clash on Wednesday.

It will be streamed via the Arsenal app and Arsenal.com, with the match taking place at the Sports Centre Radotin.

Updates will also be posted here and on the club’s Academy X page.

A full match report, highlights and post-match interviews with Horseman and one of the young players will be published on Arsenal’s official channels.

With them still in contention to qualify for the knockout stages, Arsenal will be looking to get off to a good start. Slavia’s great early start means they will have to play some of their best football of the tournament.

#2095. Total Premier League minutes played by the 8 Newcastle United signings tells a story

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

A lot of focus on the eight Newcastle United signings that were brought in across summer 2026.

There were actually nine in total but teenager Ousmane Diabate was instantly loaned back to the selling club in Turkey in order to get regular first team football.

So what about these eight Newcastle United signings that have gone straight into the first team squad?

I have had a look at the minutes played so far this 2026-27 season by the new players.

Specifically, the minutes that each of the eight have played in the Premier League.

I think it tells a very clear story now we are nine Premier League matches (810 minutes) into this 2026-27 season.

Total Premier League minutes (out of a possible total of 810) played by the eight Newcastle United signings with breakdown match by match (Ev Everton, CP Crystal Palace, AV Aston Villa, Cov Coventry, H Hull, Le Leeds, B Bournemouth, T Tottenham, Li Liverpool):

0 Ewen Jaouen (Zero minutes in any of the nine Premier League matches)

115 Bazoumana Toure (3Ev, 34AV, 30Cov, 16H, 33Le)

256 Sean Steur (26Ev, 5CP, 66H, 71 Le, 13B, 19T, 56Li)

304 Matias Fernandez-Pardo (56AV, 90Cov, 74H, 57Le, 27B)

501 Nico Gonzalez (64Ev, 90CP, 90AV, 90Cov, 19Le, 77B, 71T)

536 Amar Dedic (87Ev, 90CP, 31Cov, 58Le, 90B, 90T, 90Li)

810 Lukas Hornicek (90Ev, 90CP, 90AV, 90Cov, 90H, 90Le, 90B, 90T, 90Li)

What stands out for me, is that Matthias Jaissle has given far more Premier League minutes to the (relatively) older and more experienced Newcastle United signings.

Three of the eight Newcastle United signings were aged 24 when they arrived, the other five were 21 or under.

Lukas Hornicek has played every Premier League minute this season.

Nico Gonzalez has started all seven EPL matches he has been available for.

Amar Dedic has started all of the EPL matches he was in a position to start, as well as off the bench against Coventry.

The other five Newcastle United signings who were aged 21 and under when they arrived at St James’ Park, have played 702 minutes between them. That is less than Lukas Hornicek alone has played and would also be less than the Dedic and Gonzalez individual totals if they had been available all of the time.

On the other hand, Matias Fernandez-Pardo has played far more minutes than I think he would have otherwise done so far, simply because of so many injured players in the early part of the season.

I am sure that Matthias Jaissle sees the exciting potential of Sean Steur just as much as we do, probably even more so considering he watches him every day in training. However, my belief is that the Head coach is simply trying to protect the 18-year-old from too much expectancy and pressure too soon. The same with the other younger players.

I don’t think he is holding the likes of Toure and Bamba back from playing more minutes so far because of any negative irrational reason, more a case of giving these younger inexperienced players from weaker leagues a chance to integrate and build up their confidence rather than throwing them in at the deep end and a case of sink or swim?

The booing from many Newcastle United fans at the Everton match for example, it isn’t exactly something that will help younger players to build confidence if they are subjected to that.
